### Post #2: 2025-12-06 23:24 `[Discuss]`

**Money Amplifies Character, It Does Not Build It (Part 1)**

First off, I want to give a huge shoutout to @mphfinance for the spotlight and kind words. Beyond grateful for the support, man. And to everyone who followed after that I also want to say thank you. I don't take that trust lightly. Now I want to keep the momentum going and I will make some more chart analysis posts this weekend going into next week.

However, let's talk about something that I feel like is especially important for us as traders grinding and trying to earn “financial freedom”. Especially watching traders obsess over hitting arbitrary portfolio milestones...

Part 1: Money Is a Magnifying Glass

Money doesn't change who you are. It reveals who you've always been, just at a much larger scale.

The generous person with $5K becomes philanthropic with $5M. The insecure person buying validation with a leased BMW just upgrades to a Lamborghini they still can't afford. The disciplined trader grinding consistent 1-2% weekly gains compounds that into generational wealth. The impulsive gambler hitting a 10-bagger immediately YOLOs it into the next meme stock and blows up.

Look at lottery winners when over 70% end up bankrupt within a few years. Why? Because someone who couldn't manage $50K sure as hell can't manage $50M. The money just accelerated their existing relationship with a lack of discipline, impulse control, and financial literacy.

Meanwhile, you've got guys like Warren Buffett still living in the house he bought in 1958 for $31,500, or Chuck Feeney who gave away $8 billion and flies coach. They had those values when they had nothing. Money just gave them a bigger canvas and no I am not saying you should have millions of dollars and live broke but stay humble and genuine.

Here's what people get wrong: They think money corrupts good people or automatically makes people happier. Neither is true.

Money removes constraints. It gives you the freedom to act on who you already are without the friction of necessity holding you back. If you're fundamentally anxious, more money means more things to worry about. If you're generous at heart, more money means more capacity to help. If you're a builder, more money means bigger projects. If you're chasing validation, more money just means a bigger stage for that emptiness.

The nuance nobody talks about: While money doesn't build character in the moral sense, it does create opportunities for growth that poverty doesn't afford such as access to therapy, education, time for self-reflection, freedom from survival-mode stress. So there's a relationship, just not the direct "money = better person" equation people assume.

Bottom line: Don't chase money hoping it'll fix who you are. Work on becoming the person you want to be now, with what you have now. When money comes, you'll be ready for it.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bb79/money-amplifies-character-it-d*

### Post #4: 2025-12-07 19:17 — `$LULU, $ORCL` `[Discuss]`

**Money Amplifies Character, It Does Not Build It (Part 2)**

Going to continue my little rant from yesterday. As I have just began posting I do not have a style yet but I think on weekends I will do psychology and life advice.  There will  be a post later today about selling puts and earnings. I am looking to play $ORCL  this week as well as $LULU. Now back to the topic

Part 2: Being a Millionaire Won't Solve Your Problems

This is the hard truth traders don't want to hear: There's a threshold after which more money changes almost nothing about your day-to-day life.

Studies consistently show diminishing happiness returns as income increases past a certain threshold. After that, you're not happier—you're just maintaining the same baseline with nicer stuff.

Here's what a million dollars WON'T fix:

 Your health: Money won't make you fit, athletic, or disciplined with your body. You still have to do the reps. 

Your personality: It won't make you charismatic, funny, or interesting. If you're boring with $50K, you'll be boring with $5M just at better restaurants.

Your relationships: It won't fix the foundational cracks in your marriage. It won't rebuild your connection with your kids. If anything, it adds complexity such as prenups, resentment about who earned what, and kids who grow up entitled.

Your mental health: Anxiety, depression, imposter syndrome. Guess what none of that disappears at a certain account balance. Sometimes it gets worse because now you're stressed about losing it.

What actually matters: Most people don't need "millions." They need clarity on what they actually want.

For most people let’s say married with 2 kids, then I think it is fair to say $300K/year is a genuinely comfortable life. You're maxing retirement accounts, taking vacations, not stressing about an unexpected $5K expense, funding your kids' activities. That's a potential sweet spot where money stops being a constant concern.

But here's the thing: people say, "I want to be a millionaire" when they really mean "I want freedom, security, and respect." 

Those are achievable WAY before you hit seven figures, but they require you to actually define what they look like for YOU. 

Have a Blessed Sunday everyone 😊

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bb9k/money-amplifies-character-it-d*

### Post #11: 2025-12-09 05:13 — `$SPY` `[Discuss]`

**Why Price & Volume Are Your Real-Time Edge (While Indicators Calculate History)**

Indicators are useful tools, but they’re all calculated from past data. Price and volume show us the live auction happening right now. Understanding the difference = better timing.

The Nature of Indicators
Here’s the thing about every technical indicator including RSI, MACD, moving averages, Bollinger Bands, stochastics, etc. They’re all mathematical calculations based on historical price data.
- RSI? Uses the last 14 periods of closing prices
- 50-day MA? Averages the last 50 days
- MACD? Calculated from exponential moving averages of past prices
- Bollinger Bands? Standard deviation of historical price movement

They’re incredibly useful for context, for spotting patterns, for confirming what we are seeing. But they’re inherently lagging because they need historical data to calculate.
The market doesn’t wait for our indicators to recalculate.

The Only Real-Time Data: Price + Volume
While our indicators are processing what already happened, two things are showing us what’s happening right now:

PRICE = THE LIVE AUCTION

Every tick is the market’s current consensus on value. When $SPY prints at $685, that’s thousands of buyers and sellers agreeing on price at that exact moment.
Price movement shows us:
- Where buyers are willing to pay up
- Where sellers are willing to capitulate
- Which side has control in real-time
- When that control shifts

VOLUME = THE CONVICTION BEHIND IT

Volume tells you how much force is behind each price move.
- Price breaking resistance on heavy volume? Real breakout with participation
- Same break on light volume? Weak move, likely to fail
- Huge volume at a support level? Battle zone where institutions are positioned

Volume is real-time data showing you the intensity of the fight at each level.

The Timing Advantage
Indicators Give Us Context
They tell us:
- We are  in an uptrend (moving averages)
- Momentum is strong (MACD)
- We might be oversold (RSI)

This is valuable for the bigger picture.

Price & Volume Give Us Timing
They show us:
- The exact moment buyers step in at support
- When a breakout has real follow-through vs. when it’s failing
- Where the actual battle is happening right now
- When momentum is shifting before indicators calculate it

Indicators tell us what to watch. Price and volume tell us when to act.

How to Combine Both
Use indicators for:
- Overall trend context
- Key levels to watch
- Risk management zones

Use price & volume for:
- Exact entry timing
- Confirming your indicator signals
- Spotting traps and failed moves

Example Setup:
Indicator context: RSI oversold, price at 200-day MA support
Real-time execution: Wait for price to actually hold that support. Watch for volume spike as buyers defend. Enter when you see the defense in action, not just because indicators suggest it might happen.
That’s the difference between “it should bounce here” and “it is bouncing here.”

The Bottom Line
Indicators are powerful tools for context, planning, and risk management. They help us identify where opportunities might develop.
But price and volume show us what’s actually happening right now. They’re the live feed of the market’s auction process.

Price tells us what is happening.
Volume tells us how strong the move is.
Indicators tell us where the next move might happen. 

All three matter. But when you’re about to click that buy button, what matters most is what you’re seeing in real-time.
Learn to read the tape. The market will show you exactly what it’s doing if you watch price and volume.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bcfN/why-price-and-volume-are-your-*

### Post #13: 2025-12-09 22:40 `[Discuss]`

**Human Psychology: FOMO and Lack of Focus**

I see this a lot in the app so wanted to write a short post about it. The biggest mistake killing your account isn’t bad entries it’s not having proper focus. This is especially true of beginners starting out. You want in on everything.

You see a leap play. Jump in.
Someone posts a long-term hold. You’re in.
Earnings CSP looking juicy. Added.
Crypto pumping. Can’t miss it.
The list goes on and on.

This is how you lose.
You’re not building an edge. You’re building a portfolio of other people’s strategies that don’t fit YOUR:
- Available time
- Risk tolerance
- Capital size
- Psychological makeup

Look at the winners on this app

Every consistent trader has ONE main strategy they’ve mastered:
- Premium sellers
- Momentum breakout traders
- Earnings IV crushers
- Mean reversion traders
- Long-term value plays

When they step outside their lane? It’s calculated. Smaller size. Planned risk.
Not emotional FOMO into whatever’s trending.

The Truth About Edge
Edge isn’t found. It’s something you consistently refine.
You pick something that fits your life. You run it 50 times. You adjust. You run it 50 more times.

Patience + Consistency = Edge
Chasing + Distraction = Blown account

My lane: Weekly CSPs targeting 1% returns. Boring. Systematic. Profitable.

Your lane: TBD. But pick one and stay in it.

Stop trying to play everyone’s game. Master yours.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bckB/human-psychology-fomo-and-lack*

### Post #25: 2025-12-11 14:17

**$IRBT Short Squeeze is working ✅**

Yesterday morning I pointed out interesting volume on $IRBT hinting at a potential short squeeze. Since then as of this writing the stock is up 60% and was a beautiful play for those who took it . Now let’s talk short squeezes and understand what that means.

What Is A Short Squeeze?
A short squeeze happens when a heavily shorted stock starts rising rapidly, forcing short sellers to buy shares to close their positions (cover), which drives the price even higher. It’s a chain reaction that feeds on itself.
The Mechanics:
	1.	Stock has high short interest (lots of traders betting it goes down)
	2.	Catalyst hits - earnings beat, news, unusual volume
	3.	Price starts rising instead of falling
	4.	Shorts panic and start covering (buying to close)
	5.	Their buying creates more upward pressure
	6.	More shorts forced to cover as losses mount
	7.	Price explodes upward in a feedback loop

Key Indicators To Watch:
- High Short Interest percentage: 20%+ of float shorted = squeeze potential
- Low Float: Smaller share count = easier to move price
- Volume Spike: Unusual buying pressure starting
- Time Decay: The longer shorts hold, the more borrowing costs add up

Why $IRBT Worked:
The volume spike signaled something was shifting. When you see unusual activity on a heavily shorted name, it’s often early covering starting. Those who caught it early rode the squeeze up 60%.

The Risk:
Squeezes are violent and unpredictable. They can reverse just as fast. You need tight stops and profit-taking discipline. Don’t chase after the move is already extended.
How To Play Them:
- Enter on the volume spike before the vertical move or basically within a day if the first move since they run multiple days usually. 
- Hold shares since short squeezes are usually on dollar stocks or cheaper
- Take profits in stages as it runs
- Trail stops to protect gains
- Don’t fight the momentum once it starts

Short squeezes are some of the fastest money in the market, but they require quick decisions and disciplined exits. The real edge is spotting them BEFORE they explode.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bcsP/dollarirbt-short-squeeze-is-wo*

### Post #31: 2025-12-12 13:43 — `$UEC`

**$UEC - The secret play of 2026**

Uranium Basics and Why It Matters

Uranium's that heavy stuff used to fuel nuclear reactors, cranking out power without spitting out CO2 while they're running. It's huge for the shift to greener energy, especially with stuff like AI data centers and EVs sucking up more electricity. The U.S. government's even called uranium a critical mineral now, underscoring how vital it is for keeping our energy supply secure.

A Bit on UEC's History
UEC got its start back in 2003 as Carlin Gold Inc. in Nevada, then switched to Uranium Energy Corp in early 2005. They've grown like crazy since, becoming one of the largest and quickest-expanding uranium outfits in the States. They've got this massive database of old uranium exploration data, which has let them build out projects in the U.S., Canada, and even Paraguay.

Some key moments:

In the early 2010s, they snapped up and developed ISR projects in Texas, like Palangana and Goliad.
They've set up this hub-and-spoke setup in Wyoming and Texas to make things run smoother.
Just this past August, they fired up production again at their Wyoming hub after stocking up inventory for a while.

Right now, they've got around 171 folks on the team, and their market cap's sitting at about $6.24 billion as of mid-December 2025

How They Operate: The ISR Edge
UEC's all about finding, pulling out, and processing uranium—plus a bit of titanium here and there. Their footprint includes:

U.S. spots with full-on processing plants in Wyoming and Texas, and seven ISR projects scattered in places like New Mexico and Arizona.
Exploration up in Canada's Saskatchewan, which is loaded with uranium.
Some stuff down in Paraguay for variety.

The In-Situ Recovery (ISR) method
 Here's the gist:

Drill wells into underground uranium deposits.
Pump in a solution, like oxygenated water, to dissolve the uranium.
Suck it back up and process it topside.
It's easier on the environment, costs less, and gets going quicker, especially for the kind of deposits we have here in the U.S. Their hub-and-spoke model in Wyoming links up satellite sites to central plants, keeping everything efficient and scalable.

Fresh Updates and Money Talk
They just dropped their Q1 Fiscal 2026 results on December 10, 2025 for the period ending October 31.

Solid Finances: They're sitting on $698 million in cash, uranium inventory (valued at market), and equity holdings—no debt at all.
Inventory Boost: Holding 1.356 million pounds of U3O8, worth about $111.9 million at current prices, plus more on the way.
Cash Infusion: Wrapped up a $234 million stock offering to speed up growth, like building a new refining setup, bumping cash to around $455 million after.
Bottom Line: Posted a net loss of $10.3 million, which is pretty standard for miners gearing up like this, but way better than last year's $20.2 million loss.

On the bigger picture, UEC's getting props in the nuclear stock surge, especially with U.S. moves to ditch foreign uranium dependence and fast-track reactors. They're stoked about the critical mineral tag, which could mean more bucks and backing.

Why UEC's a Big Deal in the Energy Shift
With folks like Bank of America saying we need to triple nuclear capacity by 2050, players like UEC are key for stuff like HALEU fuel. Their eco-friendly ISR approach fits the green agenda, and deals with companies like Denison Mines set them up nicely in what could be a $10 trillion nuclear market.

### Post #35: 2025-12-13 05:21

**Understanding Earnings - The Core Metrics (Part 1)**

Most traders treat earnings like a binary event did we beat or miss, moon or dump. But if you actually understand what these numbers mean and how they’re calculated, you gain real edge. Let me break down the key metrics beyond just “revenue go up.”

The Big 3: What Companies Report
Revenue 
	•	What it is: Total money coming in the door
	•	How it’s calculated: Units sold × Price per unit
	•	Why it matters: Shows growth trajectory and market share
	•	Red flag: Revenue growing but everything else shrinking = unsustainable

Net Income 
	•	What it is: Actual profit after ALL expenses
	•	Formula: Revenue - COGS - Operating Expenses - Interest - Taxes
	•	Why it matters: This is real money the company keeps
	•	Reality check: A company can have massive revenue and still lose money (looking at you, unprofitable growth stocks)

Earnings Per Share (EPS)
	•	What it is: Net Income ÷ Total Shares Outstanding
	•	Why it matters: Most important number for stock price
	•	The game: Companies buy back shares to boost EPS without growing profit
	•	Watch for: Dilution from stock-based compensation killing EPS

What Are “Shares Outstanding” Anyway?
Total Shares Outstanding = Every single share of the company that exists right now.
When a company goes public, they create a certain number of shares. This number changes over time:

Share count goes UP from:
	•	Issuing new shares to raise money
	•	Employee stock compensation (RSUs, options)
	•	Convertible bonds converting to stock
	•	Stock splits (cosmetic - 2-for-1 doubles shares, halves price)

Share count goes DOWN from:
	•	Share buybacks (company buys and retires shares)
	•	Reverse splits (rare, usually a bad sign)
Why this matters for EPS:
Same company, same $100M profit:
	•	With 100M shares → EPS = $1.00
	•	With 50M shares → EPS = $2.00
Different EPS, same actual business performance.

How Share Buybacks Actually Work
When a company announces a buyback, here’s what happens:
- Authorization: Board approves “$10B buyback program”
- Execution: Company buys its own shares on the open market (just like you buying stock)
- Retirement: Those shares are cancelled permanently or held as “treasury stock”
- Result: Shares outstanding decreases, each remaining share owns more of the company

Think of it like pizza slices:
	•	Company profit = whole pizza
	•	Shares = number of slices
	•	Buyback = company buys slices and throws them away
	•	Same pizza, fewer slices = each slice is bigger
	•	Those shares are GONE - retired permanently
	•	Result: EPS boosted significantly even when profit growth slowed

The sneaky part: Tech companies do massive buybacks BUT also issue tons of stock to employees. So they buy back with one hand and dilute with the other. Net share count barely moves sometimes.

Always check diluted shares outstanding - includes all potential shares from options, RSUs, convertible debt. That’s the real number.

The Metrics Wall Street Actually Cares About

Operating Margin
	•	Formula: Operating Income ÷ Revenue × 100
	•	What it shows: How efficiently they turn sales into profit
	•	Good margins vary by industry:
	•	Software: 20-40% (high margin kings)
	•	Retail: 5-10% (thin margins, volume game)
	•	Manufacturing: 10-20% (middle ground)
	•	Red flag: Shrinking margins = losing pricing power or cost control

Free Cash Flow (FCF)
	•	Formula: Operating Cash Flow - Capital Expenditures
	•	Why I love it: Can’t fake cash, only earnings
	•	What it means: Actual money available for buybacks, dividends, growth
	•	Pro tip: A company can show profit but burn cash (accounting magic reverses)

Revenue Growth Rate
	•	Formula: (This Quarter Revenue - Last Year Same Quarter) ÷ Last Year × 100
	•	Why YoY matters: Removes seasonality (Q4 is always bigger for retail)
	•	Growth expectations:
	•	Mature companies: 5-10% = solid
	•	Growth stocks: 20-50% = healthy
	•	Hyper-growth: 50%+ = justify the premium
	•	Deceleration kills: Going from 40% → 30% growth tanks high-P/E stocks

The Hidden Metrics That Move Stocks

Guidance
	•	What it is: Management’s forecast for next quarter/year
	•	Why it matters MORE than the beat: Forward-looking
	•	The pattern: Beat + Lower Guidance = Stock dumps
	•	Watch for: Conservative guides who beat every quarter vs. aggressive guides who miss

Gross Margin
	•	Formula: (Revenue - Cost of Goods Sold) ÷ Revenue × 100
	•	What it shows: Profitability before operating expenses
	•	Why it matters: High gross margin = pricing power and moat
	•	Example: Apple’s 40%+ gross margin vs. Dell’s 20% = Apple’s ecosystem premium

Operating Leverage
	•	What it is: Revenue grows faster than expenses
	•	How to spot: Revenue +30%, Operating Expenses +10% = beautiful
	•	Why it matters: Path to profitability for growth companies
	•	Red flag: Expenses growing faster than revenue = burning cash

Valuation Metrics: Is It Expensive?

Price-to-Earnings (P/E) Ratio
	•	Formula: Stock Price ÷ EPS
	•	What it means: How many years of earnings you’re paying for
	•	Reality check:
	•	S&P 500 average: ~18-20x
	•	Growth stocks: 30-50x (paying for future growth)
	•	Value stocks: 10-15x (slower growth, cheaper)
	•	Forward P/E uses next year’s estimated EPS (more useful)

Price-to-Sales (P/S) Ratio
	•	Formula: Market Cap ÷ Annual Revenue
	•	When to use: Unprofitable growth companies (no P/E yet)
	•	Benchmarks:
	•	Software/SaaS: 10-15x sales = normal
	•	Retail: 0.5-2x sales = normal
	•	Red flag: P/S of 50x on a company still losing money = danger zone

PEG Ratio
	•	Formula: P/E Ratio ÷ Annual EPS Growth Rate
	•	What it shows: P/E adjusted for growth
	•	The rule: PEG < 1.0 = undervalued for its growth
	•	Example: 30 P/E with 40% growth = 0.75 PEG = potentially cheap

What Actually Moves Stocks on Earnings
The 4-Part Scorecard:
	1.	Beat or Miss EPS (most important)
	•	Consensus is on Yahoo Finance, Seeking Alpha, etc.
	•	Beat by $0.01 = usually not enough
	•	Beat by 10%+ = strong move
	2.	Beat or Miss Revenue (second most important)
	•	Revenue miss + EPS beat = cost cutting, not growth = bad
	3.	Guidance (can override the beat)
	•	Raise guidance = stock rips even on a miss sometimes
	•	Lower guidance = stock dumps even on a beat
	4.	Commentary & Trends (sentiment driver)
	•	New product cycles
	•	Market share gains/losses
	•	Margin expansion/compression
	•	Management confidence in tone


Revenue growth grabs headlines. But the real money is in understanding:
	•	Margins (profitability per dollar)
	•	Cash flow (real money being generated)
	•	Operating leverage (efficiency scaling)
	•	Guidance (future trajectory)
	•	Share count (are buybacks real or offset by dilution?)

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bcEY/understanding-earnings-the-cor*

### Post #37: 2025-12-14 03:09 — `$SPY`

**Why Higher Timeframes Trump Lower Timeframes**

Stop Fighting the Weekly Chart - Here's Why HTF Structure Matters

Quick lesson for traders getting chopped up on intraday moves: Higher timeframes always have precedence over lower timeframes. Period.
Here's what that means for your trading:

THE TIMEFRAME HIERARCHY
When there's conflict between what the 4hr chart says vs what the weekly chart says, the weekly ALWAYS wins. Think of it like this:

Weekly = The Ocean Current (unstoppable force)
Daily = The Tide (moves with the current)
4H/1H = Waves (ride the tide)
15min = Ripples (trade with the waves)

You can catch ripples going against waves for quick scalps, but eventually that ocean current pulls everything back in line.

WHY $SPY'S WEEKLY 20MA MATTERS
Historically, $SPY maintains bullish trend structure as long as price stays ABOVE the 20-period weekly MA. This isn't some magic indicator - it's a reflection of institutional positioning and long-term market structure.
Current $SPY: ~$681
Weekly 20MA: Check your charts, but we're solidly above

When SPY is above weekly 20MA:
- Primary trend = BULLISH
- Dips are buying opportunities
- Short-term bearish moves are corrections, not reversals
- Options premiums favor calls on pullbacks

When SPY breaks BELOW weekly 20MA:
- Primary trend shifts BEARISH
- Rallies become selling opportunities
- Risk management becomes critical
- Cash/hedges make sense

HOW I USE THIS
I'm not buying every dip blindly. But when SPY holds weekly 20MA, my bias is:

Buy strong dips on daily charts
Sell puts on pullbacks to support
Look for bullish reversals at key levels
Size positions larger on HTF confluence

When SPY breaks below? I flip my strategy completely.

MARKET STRUCTURE > INDICATORS
The weekly 20MA works because it shows you where large money is positioned over time. Institutions don't trade 5-min charts. They accumulate and distribute on weekly/monthly timeframes.
When you align your trades with HTF structure, you're swimming with the big money, not against it.

Bottom Line:
Stop getting shaken out of good positions because of lower timeframe noise. Zoom out. Check weekly structure. Trade with the current, not against it.

**$AVGO Earnings Breakdown - Where Management Fumbled the Call**

JPM and GS were both bullish going in. Hock Tan dropped margin compression bombs and refused to give AI guidance. Stock ate shit despite $73B AI backlog reveal.

What Went Wrong (3 Critical Mistakes)

1. MARGIN LANGUAGE WAS BRUTAL

Tan literally said gross margins would “deteriorate”and operating margins would “come down a bit” in H2 FY26.

High-multiple tech stocks ($50+ P/E) are allergic to margin compression. Using “deteriorate” vs “normalize” or “moderate” matters when you’re trading at 35x earnings. Word choice killed momentum.

2. AI CUSTOMER #5 MYSTERY + NO FY26 GUIDANCE

Announced fifth XPU customer but wouldn’t confirm if it’s OpenAI during Q&A. This screams delays or caveats.

Worse: Downplayed near-term revenue impact (“not much in ’26”) RIGHT after revealing a $73B AI backlog. Then refused to give FY26 AI guidance:

You just told me about $73B in orders but can’t guide the year? Market hates uncertainty.

3. NON-AI SOFTNESS WITH NO RECOVERY TIMELINE

61% of revenue is still semis. 39% is software. Both segments flat.

Management’s take: “We don’t see any further deterioration…but we don’t see it recovering very quickly”

Translation: AI carrying the whole company while core business stagnates indefinitely. Over-reliance on one growth driver = valuation risk.


The Real Problem

REACTIVE vs. PROACTIVE

All three issues came out during Q&A instead of prepared remarks. Management let analysts expose the cracks instead of controlling the narrative upfront.

If you know margins are compressing, say it first with context. If you can’t guide AI for FY26, explain why before they ask. If non-AI is stuck, give a timeline for inflection.

Leaving these landmines for the Q&A session made everything sound worse than it probably is.

My Take

The $73B AI backlog is real. Custom AI chips for hyperscalers = massive TAM. But Tan fumbled the delivery.

Trump floating 1% interest rates sounds bullish—but history says rapid cuts = boom then bust. Markets currently strong, but 2026 could bring volatility. Key levels to watch across all assets.

The Macro Setup

Trump mentioned wanting interest rates at 1% or lower with Kevin Walsh likely as next Fed chair. Everyone assumes cuts = moon, but the data tells a different story:

Fast cuts historically crash markets. Since the 1990s, slow cutting cycles create dull bull markets. But aggressive pivot points (like what Trump’s suggesting) create extreme booms followed by extreme busts.

Wall Street’s real plan.

### Post #41: 2025-12-16 03:10 — `$AAPL`

**The Assignment Math Nobody Talks About**

ITM covered calls can still be profitable even after assignment because premium is calculated into your cost basis. Most traders don’t know this but understanding the math changes how you think about strike selection.

The Setup

Here’s something I learned not that long ago: option assignments are always calculated post-premium. This applies to both puts AND calls.

What does that actually mean for your P&L? Let me show you with a real example.

Real Numbers - $AAPL Example

Current Price: $274 (as of 12/15/2025)

Let’s say you:

- Own 100 shares of $AAPL at $270 cost basis
- Sell the $268 covered call expiring this week
- Collect $3.00 premium** ($300 total)

Most traders think: *“Wait, if I get assigned at $268, I’m selling shares below my $270 cost basis. That’s a $2 loss per share ($200 total). Bad trade.”*

**Wrong.**

The Math

When you get assigned, your **effective sell price** is:

**Strike Price + Premium Collected**

So in this example:

- Strike: $268
- Premium: $3.00
- **Effective Sell Price: $271**

Even though you were assigned at $268 (below your $270 cost basis), **you still made money** because the premium is factored in.

Why This Matters

1. Deep ITM Covered Calls Can Still Profit

You can sell calls **below** your cost basis and still walk away green if the premium is fat enough.

This is huge for:

- Earnings plays (high IV = fat premiums)
- Volatile stocks where you want to cap upside but lock gains
- Positions you’re ready to exit anyway

2. Tax Efficiency

Since your cost basis includes the premium, your taxable gain is calculated correctly.

https://www.fidelity.com/learning-center/investment-products/options/tax-implications-covered-calls

As Fidelity explains: *“For tax purposes, when at-the-money or out-of-the-money qualified covered calls are assigned, the sale price of the stock is equal to the strike price of the call plus the net premium received for selling the call.”*

Clean. Simple. Profitable.

3. Assignment Creates a Single Transaction

Here’s the critical part: **the option and stock sale are treated as ONE transaction** for tax purposes

https://ttlc.intuit.com/community/investments-and-rental-properties/discussion/does-buying-a-stock-after-being-assigned-out-of-it-from-a-covered-call-trigger-the-wash-sale-rule/00/3075545

This means:

- You don’t separately report the option premium and stock sale
- The premium gets added to your stock proceeds
- Your broker’s 1099-B will show the combined amount
- One transaction = one gain/loss calculation

The bottom line: **if the strike + premium > your cost basis, you have a gain and wash sale doesn’t apply.** But if strike + premium < your cost basis, you have a loss and wash sale rules apply if you repurchase within 30 days.

The Catch (Because There’s Always One)

This works great if you’re okay being assigned.

What this strategy does NOT do:

- Protect you if the stock tanks hard
- Give you unlimited upside (you cap gains at strike + premium)
- Work well in low IV environments (premium is too small)

You’re trading potential upside for guaranteed premium income. Know what you’re signing up for.

Option assignments are calculated post-premium. 

This means:
Deep ITM covered calls can still be profitable
Strike + premium = your effective sale price
Tax treatment is straightforward (one combined transaction)
Wash sale rules do NOT apply if overall result is a gain
Wash sale rules DO apply if overall result is a loss

Most retail traders don’t understand how the premium affects their effective sale price. Now you do.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bcOn/the-assignment-math-nobody-tal*

### Post #43: 2025-12-17 12:45

**Use AI to Spot Risks You’re Too Biased to Notice**

At this point we all actively use AI and seek the best ways for it to make our lives easier in daily tasks including investing. I have not really seen anyone else on this app do this and since it is my daily profession decided to make a little series out of it. How AI can help us spot risks in assets that we invest into.

I’m giving away this knowledge free no paywall, no substack link. Just actionable prompts you can use today. The point is you do your research before you commit to other people’s plays on this app. They are not responsible for your losses but you are so do your own research or use Ai to do it for you 😉

Ai can have an advantage over a human investor because  a machine doesn’t get attached to a stock. It doesn’t fall in love with a CEO. It doesn’t justify red flags because “the story is still good.”

As investors, we have a tendency to ignore risks until they hit us in the face.

Here’s a 3-layer system to use AI to spot business risks:

**Layer 1**: Inside the business — the operational engine (customers, margins, growth, moat).

**Layer 2**: Behind the business — the financial foundation (liquidity, leverage, cash flow).

**Layer 3**: Around the business — the external forces (regulation, technology, industry shifts).

Each layer checks a different angle, and together they give you the full picture of what can break.

The best Ai I personally think for this job would probably be Grok as it seemed better at doing financial research then the other ones. Never tried Gemini but people say it’s good as well.

Layer 1: Core Business Risk

Layer 1 identifies what could break the business from the inside.

The AI starts by gathering real data from SEC filings, earnings transcripts, analyst reports, and industry benchmarks. Then it runs the diagnostic across four critical areas:

**Customer Risk** - Why would customers leave? The AI quantifies switching costs in actual dollars and time, models how 10% churn impacts revenue, pulls trailing 4-quarter churn data from filings, and benchmarks retention against industry averages.

**Growth Risk** - What could stop revenue growth? The AI estimates untapped TAM, tracks competitor market share trends, analyzes pipeline health from bookings data, and defines 3-5 trackable early warning KPIs like bookings growth declining below 10%.

**Margin Risk** - What could compress margins? The AI breaks down gross margin by segment, quantifies COGS inflation from supply chain trends, compares current margins to 5-year historical averages and peer medians, and flags declines over 2% YoY.

**Moat Analysis** - What is the actual moat and how could it erode? The AI quantifies switching costs, measures brand strength through pricing power, calculates scale advantages versus competitors, counts patents and R&D spend, and tracks market share trajectory as the key early warning metric.

The output is a risk matrix ranking each threat by severity (potential revenue impact %), likelihood (probability over 3 years), current evidence with sources, and management’s ability to mitigate.

Layer 2: Financial Risk Diagnostic

After checking the engine, the next step is to look behind the business at the financial foundation holding everything together.

Layer 2 asks one question every investor needs answered: “Can this company survive stress… or does one bad cycle break it?”

The AI pulls the latest 10-K/10-Q filings, debt maturity schedules, cash flow statements, credit ratings, and industry leverage benchmarks. Then it runs stress tests across six key areas:

**Liquidity** - Days cash on hand, stress scenarios showing months of runway under 10-30% revenue declines, covenant compliance status, and peer comparisons.

**Leverage** - Net debt to EBITDA and FCF ratios compared to industry median, 5-year historical range, and trajectory analysis.

**Debt Maturity** - Full maturity profile from debt schedule notes, interest rate sensitivity, and refinancing risk against current bond yields.

**Cash Flow Durability** - 5 years of FCF analysis for volatility, operating cash flow conversion percentage, and recession scenario modeling.

**Dilution** - Stock-based comp as percentage of revenue, year-over-year dilution rate, buyback spend, and ROIC on reinvested capital.

**Working Capital** - Cash conversion cycle trends, receivables aging, inventory buildups, and payables stretching.

The output is a risk matrix ranking each financial threat by severity (potential equity impact), urgency (immediate / 1-3 years / monitoring), current evidence, and management’s mitigation capacity.


Layer 3: Structural Risk (The Forces You Can’t Control)

The final step is to look around the business at the external forces that could disrupt everything.

Layer 3 asks: “What outside forces could break this business regardless of how well it executes?”

The AI searches for regulatory actions, technology threats, competitive shifts, geopolitical exposures, and macro sensitivity by examining five external categories:

**Regulatory Risk** - All applicable regulations, pending legislation and regulatory reviews, worst-case compliance costs as percentage of OpEx, revenue at risk from restrictions, and historical industry fines as precedent.

**Technology Disruption** - Current tech moat assessment, 2-4 emerging technologies that could disrupt with adoption timelines, R&D intensity versus peers, and management’s awareness from earnings calls.

**Competitive Shifts** - Current market share and top competitors, new entrant funding rounds, business model innovation, vertical integration threats, and early warning signs like ASP trends and win/loss rates.

**Geopolitical Risk** - Revenue breakdown by geography, high-risk region concentration, single-source supply chain vulnerabilities, tariff exposure modeling, and FX sensitivity with hedging coverage.

**Macro Sensitivity** - GDP beta from historical correlation, cyclical classification, interest rate impact on customer demand and debt servicing.

The output is a risk matrix ranking each structural threat by potential impact (market cap percentage loss), probability over 3 years, timeline (immediate / 1-3 years / long-term), and company’s ability to adapt.

How to Use This System

**Step 1**: Pick a stock you own or are researching.

**Step 2**: Run Layer 1 first. The AI will gather SEC filings, earnings transcripts, and analyst reports automatically.

**Step 3**: Highlight the top 3-5 risks that concern you most.

**Step 4**: Run Layer 2. The AI will pull balance sheets, debt structures, and cash flow statements to stress test the foundation.

**Step 5**: Run Layer 3. The AI will search for regulatory actions, technology threats, and geopolitical exposures.

**Step 6**: Synthesize all three layers into a single risk profile. Decide if the risk/reward makes sense.

This isn’t about avoiding all risk. It’s about knowing exactly what risks you’re taking and making sure you’re getting paid enough to take them.

Get all 3 prompts here: <https://docs.google.com/document/d/1tqREkySqB6z95v65T7xIkUergHwHURJYhNU7BxS6H8c/edit?usp=sharing>

### Post #45: 2025-12-18 14:21

**CPI vs Core CPI: What’s the Difference and Why It Matters ⬆️**

The CPI report is one of the biggest market movers every month. Let’s break down and understand the difference between headline CPI and Core CPI because the market reacts to both, but for different reasons.

What Is CPI?

The Consumer Price Index measures how much prices are changing across the economy. The Bureau of Labor Statistics tracks a huge basket of goods and services that regular people buy: food, gas, rent, healthcare, clothes, cars, entertainment, everything. Every month they check the prices and compare them to previous months.

When CPI goes up, your money buys less stuff. When it goes down, your money goes further. Simple.

The Fed watches this number obsessively because their job is keeping inflation around 2% annually. If CPI keeps running hot above that target, they keep rates high or raise them more. If CPI cools toward 2%, they can cut rates.

**Higher rates generally mean stocks go down**
**Lower rates generally mean stocks go up**

The Two CPI Numbers That Drop Each Month

Now there are actually two inflation numbers released:

**Headline CPI (Regular CPI)**
This is total inflation including EVERYTHING. Food, energy (gas prices), rent, cars, medical care, all of it. The full shopping cart.

**Core CPI (Excludes Food and Energy)**
This is the same basket but with food and energy stripped out. No groceries, no gas.

Why Remove Food and Energy?

Because those prices swing wildly month to month for reasons that have nothing to do with underlying inflation. Gas can spike 20% in one month because OPEC cuts production or a hurricane hits refineries, then drop 15% the next month when supply normalizes. Food prices bounce around based on weather, supply chain issues, bird flu outbreaks, whatever.

The Fed actually cares MORE about Core CPI because it shows the real underlying inflation trend without all that short term noise. Core CPI tells them if inflation is truly sticky in the economy or just getting bounced around by temporary shocks.

What the Fed Actually Watches

The Fed looks at both numbers but puts more weight on Core CPI for policy decisions. Here’s why:

Core CPI strips out the noise and shows them if their rate hikes are actually working to cool down demand in the economy. If Core keeps running at +0.3% or +0.4% month over month, that annualizes to roughly 3.6% to 4.8% yearly, which is way above their 2% target. That forces them to keep rates high or even raise them.

If Core CPI drops to +0.2% monthly, that annualizes closer to 2.4%, which is much closer to their target and opens the door for rate cuts.

Key Categories Inside Core CPI

When you dig into Core CPI, there are a few big drivers:

**Shelter (about 40% of Core CPI):** Rent and owners’ equivalent rent. This is huge and moves slowly. Shelter inflation has been one of the stickiest components keeping Core CPI elevated.

**Core Services:** Things like healthcare, haircuts, car insurance, airline tickets. These are sticky because they’re driven by wages. When core services cool down, it’s a strong signal that underlying inflation is decelerating.

**Core Goods:** Stuff like cars, clothes, furniture, electronics. These have been more volatile and sensitive to supply chain dynamics.

The market pays close attention to shelter because it’s such a big component and has been stubbornly high. Any signs of shelter cooling are bullish for rate cut hopes.

Bottom Line

**CPI = Total inflation (includes food and energy)**
**Core CPI = Inflation without food and energy (what the Fed focuses on)**

Core CPI matters more for Fed policy and rate cut expectations. Headline CPI matters more for consumer sentiment and what you feel at the pump and grocery store

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bc0H/cpi-vs-core-cpi-whats-the-diff*

### Post #46: 2025-12-18 15:56 — `$MU, $ASX, $NVT, $VIAV, $FIX, $MOD, $AMKR, $CSCO, $CIEN, $VRT, $ANET, $COHR, $AAOI, $FLNC, $BE, $LITE, $CEG, $TTM`

**AI Infrastructure: The 2025-2030 Mega Trend ⬆️**

The AI gold rush isn’t slowing down. It’s evolving. While everyone’s focused on who builds the best models, the real money is in the picks and shovels. Here’s what’s actually constraining AI growth and where the bottlenecks create opportunity.

The Core Constraints

Power is the #1 bottleneck. Data centers are scaling from 100 MW to 1 GW facilities. You can’t run AI at scale without solving power delivery, distribution, and generation.

Cooling becomes critical as GPU TDP explodes. When you’re packing chips that consume 700W+ each into dense racks, traditional air cooling dies. Liquid cooling, immersion cooling, and advanced thermal management go from “nice to have” to “must have.”

PCB shortages for GPUs and ASICs. High layer count printed circuit boards with advanced materials (low loss, high frequency) are already constrained. As chip complexity grows, PCB manufacturing becomes a chokepoint.

Storage and memory spike. Inference workloads and video/image generation are memory intensive. Training was about compute. Inference is about moving massive amounts of data fast. Storage demand isn’t linear. It’s exponential.

Optics is the next memory. As data centers scale to gigawatt facilities, moving data between racks, buildings, and campuses requires high speed optical interconnects. 400G, 800G, 1.6T optics aren’t optional. They’re the backbone. Optics is becoming as critical as memory was 10 years ago.

The Play

The alpha is in finding top suppliers for each constraint layer. The companies that supply the non-commoditized, high value components in power delivery, cooling systems, advanced PCBs, high bandwidth memory, and optical transceivers.

Some leaders are public:

**Optical Interconnects**: $COHR $CIEN $LITE $VIAV $ANET $CSCO $AAOI

**Cooling Systems**: $VRT $MOD $NVT

**Memory & Packaging**: $MU $ASX $AMKR

**Power & Infrastructure**: $CEG

**Other Key Players**: $TTM $FLNC $BE $FIX

But many top suppliers are still private. Do the work. Map the supply chain. Find who’s actually selling into the hyperscalers and AI infrastructure builders.

### Post #47: 2025-12-19 02:36

**Japan Carry Trade: What’s Happening & Why It Matters**

Japan’s raising rates after 30 years of near-zero policy. This changes the math on a major global trading strategy, which could create both opportunities and volatility across markets.

What Is the Carry Trade?

The carry trade is straightforward: borrow money in a currency with low interest rates, invest it where returns are higher, keep the spread.

For decades, investors borrowed Japanese yen at near-zero rates, converted to dollars, and invested in higher-yielding US bonds, stocks, or other assets. The difference between Japan’s 0% and US rates around 4-5% was the profit.

This trade has been massive. Trillions have flowed from Japan into global markets using this strategy, providing significant liquidity to asset prices worldwide.

What’s Changing in December 2025

**Japan Is Raising Rates**

The Bank of Japan is hiking its key rate to levels not seen in roughly 30 years. When Japan’s rates rise, the gap between borrowing costs and foreign returns narrows. This makes the traditional carry trade less profitable.

**The Yen Is Adjusting**

Higher rates typically strengthen a currency. A stronger yen means repaying yen-denominated loans becomes more expensive in dollar terms.

Example: Borrowing ¥1M when $1 = ¥150 versus repaying when $1 = ¥140 means you need more dollars to cover the same yen amount.

**Position Adjustments Are Happening**

Some investors are reducing carry trade exposure as the risk/reward profile changes. This means selling certain foreign assets and buying back yen to close positions.

What This Could Mean for Markets

**Liquidity Shifts**: The carry trade has been a significant source of capital flowing into global risk assets. Changes in this flow could affect market dynamics, creating both headwinds and tailwinds depending on positioning.

**Potential Volatility**: Large position adjustments can create price movement. We’ve seen carry trade concerns contribute to market swings in the past (like August 2024). This could also create entry opportunities in quality assets.

**Market Rotation**: As some strategies become less attractive, capital may rotate to others. Defensive plays might see increased interest, but growth assets with strong fundamentals could still perform well.

Potential Scenarios

**Bull Case**: Orderly transition, capital rotates to quality US assets, strong companies continue performing

**Bear Case**: Rapid unwind creates volatility spike, leveraged positions get pressured

**Most Likely**: Gradual adjustment with periodic volatility, creating both challenges and opportunities

Bottom Line

The carry trade represents a real shift in global capital flows as Japan changes policy after 30 years. This affects market structure whether you trade currencies directly or not.

This isn’t necessarily bullish or bearish. It’s a change in the underlying dynamics. Changes create opportunities for those paying attention.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bc5k/japan-carry-trade-whats-happen*

### Post #48: 2025-12-19 15:21 — `$SPY`

**Quad Witching: If you never heard of this word then this post is for you 🧠**

Today is Quad Witching, the third Friday of December when four types of derivatives expire simultaneously. It’s the day you usually see wild price swings and massive volume especially towards the later end of the day.

**WHAT IS IT?**

Four derivative products all expire at market close today:

• Stock index futures
• Stock index options  
• Single stock futures
• Single stock options

This happens four times per year: third Friday of March, June, September, and December.

**What happens from Institutional Perspective**

Institutions hold massive positions in these derivatives. As expiration hits, they’re forced to either roll positions forward or settle. This creates enormous volume, often 30-50% higher than normal.

Market makers who sold options need to hedge by buying or selling underlying stocks. The result? Sudden buying or selling pressure that can move stocks significantly into the close.

**WHAT TO EXPECT**

The chaos peaks in the last 60-90 minutes of trading:

- Volume explosion starting around 3pm ET
- Increased volatility and wider spreads  
- “Pinning” where stocks gravitate toward major strike prices
- Rapid reversals as large positions unwind

**THE PIN EFFECT**

Stocks often get “pinned” to strikes with massive open interest. If $SPY has 1M contracts at the $680 strike, market makers want to keep it near that level. Once expiration hits at 4pm, that gravitational pull disappears.

**PRACTICAL IMPACT**

If you’re holding weeklies expiring today, expect increased slippage and potential violence. The combination of institutional repositioning and algorithmic hedging creates price action that has nothing to do with fundamentals.

Many intraday traders avoid the final hour entirely or trade smaller size. Swing traders often ignore this as noise.

**AFTER THE STORM**

Monday typically returns to normal volume and price action. The pinning effects and hedging pressure evaporate. Stocks move more freely based on actual supply and demand.

**KEY TAKEAWAY**

Quad witching is mechanical, not fundamental. The wild swings aren’t telling you anything about real market direction. It’s just the derivatives market doing its quarterly reset.

By Monday morning, we’re back to normal market structure.

Not financial advice. Just teaching you something people mention but never actually  explain

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bc6U/quad-witching-if-you-never-hea*

### Post #49: 2025-12-20 23:07

**Navigating Trading Taxes: Capital Gains, TTS, Section 475, and LLC Benefits 💰💰💰**

This is a very long and detailed post that took me most of today to write, verify, and fact check. I ask to the large accounts of the AH community @Anya @mphinance @MarketVictor @TRON @TerrifiedOfAI and others - if you feel this is good and useful Information please repost. End of the year is here and tax literacy is crucial for traders to understand. Help spread the knowledge 🧠. For transparency I personally am not a TTS although I do have several LLCs I use for write offs.  Now let’s get to the post. 

—————————————————————

Trading can be a thrilling pursuit, but when tax season rolls around, it often turns into a complex puzzle. Whether you’re a day trader, swing trader, or somewhere in between, understanding how the IRS treats your gains and losses is crucial for minimizing your tax bill. In this educational blog post, we’ll break down the differences between capital gains and ordinary income (often misphrased as “regular gains”), explain how to qualify for Trader Tax Status (TTS) and make a Section 475 election to convert capital treatment to ordinary, and explore the advantages of setting up an LLC to write off trading expenses. We’ll draw on established tax principles, focusing on benefits for business traders as opposed to casual investors.

This guide is based on U.S. tax rules as of late 2025, including updates from the IRS and recent legislation like the One Big Beautiful Bill Act. Remember, tax laws evolve, and this isn’t personalized advice. You should also do your own research and consult a tax professional for your situation.

Capital Gains vs. Ordinary Income: The Basics

When you sell an asset like stocks, options, or cryptocurrencies, the profit (or loss) is typically classified as a capital gain or loss. However, traders who qualify for special status can shift this to ordinary income treatment, which has its own pros and cons.

**Capital Gains**: These apply to profits from assets held as investments. They’re divided into short-term (held for one year or less) and long-term (held more than one year). Short-term gains are taxed at your ordinary income tax rates, which for 2025 range from 10% to 37% depending on your taxable income bracket. Long-term gains enjoy preferential rates: 0%, 15%, or 20% for most taxpayers, plus a potential 3.8% Net Investment Income Tax (NIIT) for higher earners. This makes holding investments long-term attractive for tax savings, but it’s not ideal for active traders who buy and sell frequently. For 2025, the 0% rate applies to taxable income up to $48,350 (single) or $96,700 (married filing jointly); 15% up to $533,400 (single) or $600,050 (joint); and 20% above that.

**Ordinary Income**: Also called “regular gains” in casual terms, this is taxed at the same progressive rates as your wages or business income (10% to 37%). Unlike capital gains, there’s no preferential rate for long-term holdings. However, ordinary losses can offset any type of income without limits (subject to other rules like excess business loss limitations), making it powerful for deducting big trading losses.

When comparing the two treatments, the key differences are: **Tax rates** are 10-37% for short-term capital gains and 0-20% plus NIIT for long-term capital gains, while ordinary income is taxed at 10-37% with no long-term discount. **Loss treatment** limits capital losses to $3,000 net loss against ordinary income with excess carrying forward, whereas ordinary losses are fully deductible against any income and can create NOL carryforwards. **Applicability to traders** defaults to capital gains for securities, futures, and crypto, while ordinary income is available via TTS plus Section 475 election. **Wash sale rules** apply to capital gains (deferring losses if repurchased within 30 days) but are exempt under Section 475.

In essence, capital gains favor passive investors, while ordinary treatment benefits active traders by allowing fuller loss deductions, especially if you rack up losses in volatile markets.

Achieving Trader Tax Status (TTS)

To shift from investor to business trader treatment, you need TTS. This isn’t an election you file. It’s  a facts-and-circumstances determination based on your trading activity. The IRS considers you a trader if your activity is substantial, continuous, and regular, with the intent to profit from short-term market swings rather than long-term appreciation or dividends.

Key requirements include:

**Frequency and Volume**: Trading activity must be substantial, regular, frequent, and continuous, often interpreted as hundreds of trades per year, with trades on most business days.

**Holding Periods**: Mostly short-term (days or weeks, not months).

**Time Spent**: Treat it like a full-time job. This means several hours daily on research, execution, and monitoring.

**Dollar Amount**: Significant capital at risk, with trades totaling substantial sums relative to your portfolio.

**No Other Job**: If trading is your primary income source, it strengthens your case.

TTS unlocks business expense deductions (e.g., home office, software, data fees, education) on Schedule C, avoiding the suspension of investment expenses under the Tax Cuts and Jobs Act (TCJA) through 2025. It also paves the way for a Section 475 election. You can claim TTS retroactively, even amending prior returns up to three years back, but document your activity meticulously.

Section 475 Mark-to-Market (MTM) Election: Converting to Ordinary Treatment

Think of TTS as your undergraduate degree and Section 475 as grad school. It builds on TTS to treat your gains and losses as ordinary. Under MTM, you “mark” your positions to market value at year-end, reporting unrealized gains/losses as if sold. This election applies only to securities and commodities (not crypto or ETNs).

**Benefits**:

Converts capital gains/losses to ordinary, bypassing the $3,000 capital loss limit.

Exempts you from wash sale rules. No more deferred losses from repurchasing within 30 days.

Ordinary losses offset any income, potentially creating a net operating loss (NOL) carryforward (limited to 80% of future taxable income post-2025).

Qualifies for the 20% Qualified Business Income (QBI) deduction on net trading income (after expenses), though phased out for high earners.

**How to Elect**:

For individuals with TTS: File a statement with your 2025 tax return (due April 15, 2026) to elect for 2026. If you’re late for 2025, you’re stuck with capital treatment this year.

For new entities (e.g., LLC): Elect within 75 days of formation.

You can revoke it later if needed, but under new IRS rules, revocations are locked for 5 years; it’s irrevocable for the elected year.

Caution: Section 475 applies to all your trading positions, so separate investments in a different account if you want long-term capital gains. It’s ideal for consistent losers or those with big wash sales, but less so if you have net gains (since you lose long-term rates).

Forming an LLC for Your Trading Business

Setting up an LLC can elevate your trading from a hobby to a bona fide business, amplifying tax benefits. An LLC provides limited liability protection (shielding personal assets from trading debts) and flexible taxation.

**Steps to Form and Use an LLC**:

**Register**: File articles of organization in your state (costs $50–$500). Get an EIN from the IRS.

 **Tax Election**: Default is pass-through (like a sole prop or partnership), but elect S-corp status for bigger perks.

**Fund and Trade**: Transfer capital to the LLC’s brokerage account. Track expenses separately.

**Tax Advantages**:

**Expense Write-Offs**: Deduct trading-related costs like monitors, internet, and travel to seminars as business expenses on Schedule C or the entity return. Casual investors can’t fully deduct these under current tax rules.

**Health Insurance and Retirement**: With an S-corp election, pay yourself a reasonable salary to deduct health insurance premiums and contribute more to retirement plans like a Solo 401(k). This includes employee deferrals and employer profit-sharing, with higher limits than standard IRAs.

**SALT Cap Workaround**: In about 30 states, make pass-through entity (PTE) payments to deduct state and local taxes as a business expense, bypassing the federal SALT deduction cap (recently increased under new legislation).

**QBI Deduction**: The Qualified Business Income (QBI) deduction is a tax break for pass-through businesses (like LLCs), allowing you to deduct up to 20% of your net qualified business income, such as Section 475 gains minus expenses. It’s designed to give small businesses a tax advantage similar to corporate rates, but it phases out for higher earners and has special rules for service-based trades like trading (e.g., wage and property limits apply during phase-out).

**Loss Flexibility**: Consolidate trading in the entity to avoid mixing with personal investments, perfecting TTS and Section 475 use.

An LLC with S-corp election is often best for couples or solos seeking these deductions, but it adds admin (payroll, filings). If you’re not qualifying for TTS, an LLC alone won’t magically create business treatment.

Handling Losses: Wash Sales, Limitations, and Strategies

Losses are inevitable in trading, but tax rules dictate how you deduct them.

**Wash Sales**: If you sell at a loss and buy substantially identical securities within 30 days before/after (a 61-day window), the loss is deferred and added to the new position’s basis. This creates “phantom” gains later. Break the chain by selling out completely at year-end and waiting 31 days. Note: Wash sales do not apply to cryptocurrencies, as they are not securities.

**Capital Loss Limit**: Without TTS/475, net losses are capped at $3,000 against ordinary income; excess carries forward.

**Excess Business Loss (EBL)**: For 2025, non-corporate taxpayers can’t deduct business losses exceeding $313,000 (single) or $626,000 (married), adjusted for inflation. Excess becomes an NOL.

With TTS and Section 475, losses are ordinary and unlimited (post-EBL), exempt from wash sales. For Section 1256 contracts (futures), you can carry back losses three years or keep 60/40 capital gains treatment.

Quarterly Estimated Taxes: Pay as You Go to Avoid Penalties (ELI5 Version)

Trading profits can come in unpredictable bursts. A trade in February might net you $30K, but unlike a salaried job with automatic withholding, the IRS doesn’t get its cut upfront. This is where quarterly estimated taxes come in: Think of it as the IRS saying, “Pay us throughout the year as you earn, or we’ll ding you with penalties.” For traders, whose income swings wildly with the market, nailing this is key to avoiding nasty surprises. Let’s break it down simply, with practical tips tailored for traders.

The Basic Problem

The IRS wants its money as you make it, not in one lump sum on April 15. If you’re raking in trading gains (capital or ordinary via TTS/Section 475), especially without a W-2 job withholding taxes, you’ll likely need to send payments four times a year. This prevents you from “borrowing” from Uncle Sam interest-free.

The Rules (Super Simple)

You generally need to pay quarterly estimated taxes if:

You’ll owe at least $1,000 in federal taxes for the year after credits and withholding.

Your withholding (e.g., from a part-time job or spouse’s salary) and credits won’t cover at least 90% of your total tax bill.

For traders, this is common because brokerage accounts don’t withhold taxes on gains like employers do on paychecks. The tax year is divided into four uneven “quarters” (not calendar quarters): **Q1** covers the income period from January 1 through March 31 with payment due April 15, 2025; **Q2** covers April 1 through May 31 with payment due June 16, 2025 (June 15 was a Sunday in 2025, so shifted to next business day); **Q3** covers June 1 through August 31 with payment due September 15, 2025; and **Q4** covers September 1 through December 31 with payment due January 15, 2026.

Pro Tip for Traders: If your big wins happen early (e.g., Q1 market rally), pay up by April to avoid penalties accruing all year. If income is lumpy, use the “annualized income installment method” on Form 2210 to calculate payments based on actual quarterly earnings instead of even splits.

What Happens If You Don’t Pay?

The IRS hits you with an underpayment penalty, which is basically interest on the amount you should’ve paid but didn’t. It’s calculated like loan interest:

**Rate**: 7% per year for 2025, compounded daily (meaning it grows a tiny bit each day on the growing balance).

**When It Starts**: The day after the quarterly deadline.

**How It’s Calculated**: Using Form 2210 (or the IRS does it for you). Even if you get a refund overall, you can still owe penalties for late quarterly payments.

**Example for a Trader**:

You score a $100K trading profit in March 2025 (Q1).

Assuming a 30% effective tax rate (federal + state), you owe about $30K on that chunk.

If you skip the April 15 payment, from April 16, you’re charged 7% annual interest on the unpaid portion, compounding daily.

By year-end, that could add $1,000–$1,500 in penalties, depending on when you pay up. If you wait until April 2026, it’s even more.

For traders with volatile income, penalties can pile up if you underestimate during a hot streak and overpay later—better to overpay slightly and get a refund.

How to Avoid the Penalty: Safe Harbor Rules

The IRS gives “get out of jail free” options. Meet one, and no penalty, even if you underpaid a bit:

 **90% Rule**: Pay at least 90% of your *current year’s* total tax liability through withholding and estimates, spread across quarters.

OR

 **Prior Year Rule**: Pay 100% of your *prior year’s* (2024) total tax liability (or 110% if your 2024 AGI was over $150,000 or $75,000 if married filing separately). This is easier if your income is rising, as you base it on known numbers.

Practical Steps for Traders to Handle Estimated Taxes

**Estimate Your Tax**: Use IRS Form 1040-ES worksheets or software like TurboTax/TaxAct. Factor in trading gains/losses, self-employment tax (if TTS), NIIT, and state taxes. Track trades monthly to forecast.

**Pay Easily**: You can pay online using IRS Direct Pay (free, instant) or EFTPS (set up account for recurring), via the IRS2Go app for mobile payments, by mail with a check and Form 1040-ES voucher, or by credit card (though fees apply around 2%, so avoid unless desperate for rewards).

**Special Trader Hacks**:

**If you have a day job, increase W-4 withholding to cover trading taxes**

If you have a regular job (with a W-2 paycheck), you can tell your employer to take out extra taxes from your salary each paycheck. You do this by updating your Form W-4 (the withholding form you fill out for your job). This extra withholding can cover taxes on your trading profits, even though trading isn’t part of your job income. The IRS treats this withholding as if it were paid evenly all year, so it counts toward your estimated tax requirements. It lets you skip the hassle of calculating and mailing separate quarterly estimated payments (using Form 1040-ES). Instead, the money comes out automatically from your paycheck. This is great for traders who forget deadlines or hate extra paperwork. Use the IRS Tax Withholding Estimator tool to figure out how much extra to withhold. Then, submit a new W-4 to your boss. For example, if you expect $10,000 in trading taxes, you could spread that as extra withholding over your paychecks.

**For Q4: Skip the Jan 15 payment if you file your full return and pay any balance by Jan 31, 2026.**

The fourth quarterly estimated tax payment is normally due on January 15 (for income from September to December). But if you file your full tax return early by January 31 of the next year and pay any remaining taxes owed at that time, you can skip making that separate January payment altogether. It gives you a bit more time to calculate your exact taxes (since you have the full year’s info by then) and avoids an extra payment step. This is especially useful if your Q4 trading was unpredictable or if you want to bundle everything into one filing. Note: This only works if you file by Jan 31 and pay up otherwise, penalties kick in. Prepare your 2025 tax return (e.g., Form 1040) early in January 2026. Use software like TurboTax or a CPA to file electronically by Jan 31, and pay any balance owed via direct debit or check. If you’re getting a refund, even better then no payment needed!

**Variable Income? Use the annualized method: Calculate tax based on actual income each quarter (e.g., pay more in Q1 if that’s when you profited big).**

If your trading income isn’t steady (e.g., big wins in one quarter, losses in another), the standard way assumes even income all year, which might cause penalties if you underpay early on. The “annualized income installment method” lets you calculate each quarterly payment based on the actual income you earned up to that point. You “annualize” it by projecting what your full-year income would look like based on that quarter’s earnings, then pay taxes accordingly. It matches your payments to when you actually made money, so you avoid penalties for uneven income. For example, if you had a huge Q1 profit but slim pickings later, you’d pay more in April (Q1 deadline) but less in later quarters. This is perfect for traders with volatile markets. Use Form 2210 (Underpayment of Estimated Tax by Individuals) and its Schedule AI when filing your taxes. Fill it out to show your income per period (e.g., Jan-Mar for Q1). Tax software often handles this automatically if you input your quarterly earnings.

**Overpay Intentionally: If markets are hit get a refund or credit toward next year.**

If you’re having a great trading year (markets are “hot”), estimate your taxes a bit higher than needed and pay more each quarter. At tax time, if you’ve overpaid, the IRS will refund the extra (or apply it as a credit to next year’s taxes). There’s no penalty for overpaying and it’s safer than underpaying and getting hit with interest (currently around 7%). Plus, a refund feels like a bonus, and it avoids stress if your estimates were off. The downside? You’re basically giving the IRS an interest-free loan until refund time, so don’t overdo it if you need the cash now. When calculating your quarterly estimates (using Form 1040-ES), round up your projected income or tax rate. If you overpay, choose “refund” or “apply to next year” on your return.

**Tools: IRS Withholding Estimator or trader-specific software like TradeLog for gain tracking.**

The IRS Withholding Estimator is an online calculator on that helps figure out how much to withhold from your paycheck or estimate payments. TradeLog is specialized software for traders. It tracks your trades, calculates gains/losses, and generates tax reports (like Form 8949 for capital gains). They make complex calculations easy and accurate, reducing errors that could lead to penalties. The IRS tool is free and official.

Advanced Considerations

**Instrument-Specific Rules**: Securities default to capital; Section 1256 gets 60/40 (60% long-term); forex ordinary unless elected otherwise; crypto like securities but no 475/wash sales.

**Retirement Plans**: TTS S-corps allow Solo 401(k) contributions based on year-end profits.

**Entities Beyond LLC**: Partnerships for spouses to segregate trading/investments.

Wrapping Up

Business traders fare better than investors by leveraging TTS, Section 475, and entities like LLCs to deduct expenses, handle losses efficiently, and access deductions like QBI and SALT workarounds. If you’re trading actively, assess your eligibility now, especially with 2025 wrapping up. Always back up claims with records, and seek expert guidance to avoid IRS scrutiny.

Happy trading, and may your gains be tax-efficient!

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bdbH/navigating-trading-taxes-capit*

The Santa Claus Rally Reality

**Top 10 Sectors to Watch Going Into 2026**

Here are my picks for ipositioning based on recent industry analysis and market projections:

**1. AI/Semiconductors**
The foundation of everything. AI is projected to add trillions to global GDP by 2030, with 2026 marking critical mass adoption across industries. Chip demand is exploding—$500B committed globally to triple semiconductor capacity by 2032. Data centers, specialized AI chips, and edge computing are the key drivers.

**2. Energy (Clean + Traditional)**
Dual opportunity: renewables scaling aggressively (solar, wind, green hydrogen) while natural gas and nuclear fill the baseload gap. AI data centers alone are creating a generational shift in power demand. Decarbonization policies + energy security = sustained investment cycle.

**3. Biotech**
Aging demographics, personalized medicine, AI-driven drug discovery, and telemedicine expansion post-pandemic. The convergence of biotech innovation with AI diagnostics is unlocking new treatment paradigms. Preventive care and bioprocessing are growth engines.

**4. Aerospace/Space**
Space economy projected to hit $1T by 2030. Reusable rockets, satellite constellations, and commercial applications are no longer sci-fi. Defense spending + recovering air traffic + digital manufacturing = multiyear runway. AI-optimized production cutting costs.

**5. Industrial Automation**
Advanced manufacturing up 25% driven by robotics, AI, and smart supply chains. Reshoring trends accelerating as companies prioritize resilience. Agentic AI reducing downtime and optimizing production. The factory of the future is being built now.

**6. Cybersecurity**
Threat landscape exploding with AI-powered attacks and 40B IoT devices by 2030. Quantum-resistant encryption becoming critical. Every sector such as finance, manufacturing, healthcare are ramping investment. Edge AI and post-quantum crypto are the new battlegrounds.

**7. Fintech**
Projected Market surpassing $324B globally by 2026. Digital banking, DeFi, mobile payments, and regulatory tailwinds for financial inclusion. AI integration improving fraud detection and credit decisions. Cloud infrastructure enabling scalability.

**8. AgriTech**
Food security + climate challenges driving precision farming, AI optimization, and sustainable practices. Automation and biotech integration addressing labor shortages and yield improvement. Emerging sector with strong government support.

**9. Mobility (EVs/Batteries)**
EV adoption accelerating with falling costs, government incentives, and emissions regulations. Battery technology improvements and charging infrastructure expansion creating network effects. Supply chains maturing.

**10. Quantum Computing**
Projected Market reaching $72B by 2035, but commercial applications arriving sooner. Optimization, cryptography, drug discovery, and materials science breakthroughs. Hardware scaling challenges being solved. Early movers capturing mindshare.

**The Interconnected Thesis**

These sectors aren’t independent. AI powers biotech drug discovery and energy grid optimization. Semiconductors enable quantum and cybersecurity. Clean energy fuels EV adoption and data centers. Industrial automation depends on cybersecurity and AI.

**Bottom Line**: 2026 won’t be about one sector—it’s about the convergence of these trends. Position across multiple themes to capture the transformation. The companies bridging these sectors (AI + Energy, Fintech + Cybersecurity, Biotech + AI) have asymmetric upside.

### Post #53: 2025-12-25 14:55

**The Only Trade That Matters This Week 🎄🎅**

I’m going to be out the rest of this week. Taking a break from the screen to focus on something that actually compounds: time with the people I love.

**Here’s the truth we all know but forget:**

Money comes and goes. We chase it, we lose it, we make it back. The numbers in our accounts go up and down. But the memories we make? Those stay forever.

Your parents getting older every time you see them. Your kids growing up faster than you realize. That conversation with an old friend that reminds you who you used to be. The laughter around the dinner table that makes your face hurt. These are one-time opportunities. No replays. No second chances.

**What I’ve learned:**

We tell ourselves we’re working hard to provide for our families, to give them a better life. And that’s real. But somewhere along the way, we can forget that presence is the greatest gift we can give. Not our potential future wealth. Not what we’ll be able to afford someday. Just… being there. Actually listening. Fully engaged.

The holidays remind us what truly matters. It’s not about what you achieve or accumulate. It’s about who you share it with. The moments that make you feel grateful to be alive.

**My focus this rest of the week:**

Being present. Phone down. Actually listening when people talk. Soaking in the small moments that become the big memories years from now.

Making time for the people who’ve always made time for me.

Remembering that success without connection is just… empty.

** To everyone in this community:**

You’ve helped me grow as a trader this year. Thank you all for the support. 

But close your app for a day. Hug your people. Make the memories. The market will wait.

Monday we’re back to business. But today? We’re remembering why we do this in the first place.

Wishing you all a holiday filled with the kind of wealth that doesn’t show up in your portfolio…..the kind that shows up in your heart.

See you next week. 🙏

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bduL/the-only-trade-that-matters-th*
