### Post #54: 2025-12-27 16:03 — `$MU, $AMD, $MRVL, $ALAB, $ZETA`

**AI Infrastructure Power 5: My High Conviction Plays for 2026**

Last week I dropped my top 10 sectors for 2026. Now I’m going sector by sector with my actual top 5 picks in each one. Starting with AI Infrastructure because this is where the real money is getting deployed right now.

After cutting out the way too high beta noise, here’s my refined basket (Note there are dozens of good stocks but I tried to pick my best for 5 overall): **AMD, MRVL, MU, ALAB, ZETA**. This lineup hits the entire AI stack from silicon to connectivity to software with a mix of proven scale and emerging breakouts.

These are high beta bets on AI’s next wave: custom accelerators, memory bottlenecks, rack scale networking, and AI native applications. Volatility will be there but the growth potential justifies the risk if you can stomach the swings through 2026’s AI capex cycle.

The Picks (Deep Dive)

1. $AMD  ~$215

Financials: Market cap ~$350B, TTM revenue $32B, profit margin 10%, ROE 5%, low debt. Trailing P/E high at ~113x (reflecting explosive growth), but forward P/E ~35x and PEG ~0.48 signal value for expected growth.

**Why I’m in:** MI400 series confirmed for 2026 on CDNA 5 arch, with claims of strong performance (e.g., competitive vs. Nvidia’s post-Blackwell Rubin in some metrics; beats current Blackwell in inference for certain workloads). Data center momentum continues. Custom silicon pipeline with Microsoft and Meta confirmed gives defensible revenue beyond merchant chips. Analysts mostly Buy, average target ~$283 (30%+ upside).

**The risks:** Nvidia’s CUDA moat is still massive. Any hyperscaler capex slowdown hits AMD harder given smaller scale. Export controls could cap China TAM. Trading at 21x forward earnings so execution needs to be perfect.

**My take:** This is my anchor position in the basket. Proven business model, diversifying revenue, strong balance sheet. Heaviest weight here for long term AI share gains.

-----

 2. $MRVL  ~$86

Financials: Market cap ~$74B, TTM revenue ~$7.8B, strong profit margin ~32%, ROE 18%. Forward P/E ~24x reasonable for growth.

**Why I’m in:** AI networking is massively underpriced right now. Marvell’s custom ASIC business with Amazon and Google is ramping production. AI networking and optics running 40%+ CAGR through 2026. Their connectivity wins position them for multi-year data center ramps. Dependency on hyperscalers adds volatility but undervalued vs peers.

**The risks:** Revenue concentrated in 3-5 hyperscalers creates quarterly lumpiness. Broadcom still dominates custom silicon so Marvell needs flawless execution.

**My take:** This is the picks and shovels play. Every AI data center needs Marvell’s plumbing. Undervalued relative to growth profile. Medium heavy allocation for connectivity leverage.

-----

 3. $MU  ~$285

Financials: Market cap ~$321B, TTM revenue $42B (massive scale-up), profit margin ~28%, ROE 23%. Forward P/E ~9x and PEG ~0.5 scream value—cheapest in the basket by far.

**Why I’m in:** AI memory supercycle is real and in full swing. Micron’s HBM is sold out through 2026. AI memory bottleneck is THE supply constraint for scaling training clusters. DRAM pricing inflected positive, NAND stabilizing after 2 year down cycle. Gross margins expanding from low 20s toward high 30s equals massive EPS explosion. Analysts strongly Buy, targets up to $500, average ~$300.

**The risks:** Cyclical pricing is the killer. Any demand softening crushes ASPs and margins instantly. Memory oversupply could return late 2026 if China floods the market.

**My take:** Highest conviction despite the recent run. Overweighting MU for asymmetric setup even at current levels. AI memory supercycle supports conviction. Heavy allocation but sized for the volatility.

-----

4. $ALAB  ~$170

Financials: Market cap ~$28B, TTM revenue ~$723M (small base), profit margin ~28%. Forward P/E ~71x premium reflects hypergrowth expectations.

**Why I’m in:** AI rack scale connectivity breakout story. Astera’s CXL controllers and PCIe retimers are becoming critical for AI server architectures. Went public March 2024 and ramping production with Nvidia/AMD platforms. Triple digit revenue growth off small base with strong IP moat shows execution upside.

**The risks:** Post IPO volatility, stock swings on any quarter. Customer concentration likely heavy Nvidia ecosystem creates lumpiness. Trading at premium multiples requires perfect hypergrowth.

**My take:** Speculative but yes. This is the emerging breakout play. Small enough to multiple if thesis works but volatile. Smaller allocation for asymmetric upside. Not for weak hands.

-----

5. $ZETA  ~$19

Financials: Market cap ~$5B, TTM revenue ~$1.22B. Still slight net losses TTM, but adjusted EBITDA positive/growing (~21-22% margins guided). Forward P/E ~21x.

**Why I’m in:** Actually profitable AI software play with re-rating potential. This is the anti speculation trade in the basket. Already profitable today with 20%+ EBITDA margins generating real cash flow. Their AI powered customer data platform (1B+ consumer profiles) uses ML for predictive marketing. Recent GenAI product launches (AI copywriting, campaign optimization) driving ASP expansion. Net dollar retention 120%+ showing sticky enterprise contracts.

**The risks:** Economic sensitivity, ad spend slowdowns hit marketing tech first. Competition from CRM giants (Salesforce, Adobe) integrating AI could commoditize the moat. GenAI monetization execution needs to accelerate. Small cap liquidity creates volatility.

**My take:** Buying for AI software exposure with downside protection. This balances the semiconductor cyclicality. ZETA generates cash TODAY and benefits from AI tailwinds without chip supply chain risks. Medium weight for diversification within the AI theme.

## The Overall Play

This basket hits the full AI stack with 25-50% average revenue growth. Betas are extreme and volatility will be there but that’s the price of admission for AI infrastructure exposure.

**How I would Enter:** Scaling in over 4-8 weeks to average volatility and expecting a pullback in January / Feb. Using 10-15% pullbacks to add to core positions (AMD/MU/MRVL).

Next up: Energy sector breakdown.

### Post #55: 2025-12-27 22:15 — `$OKLO, $CCJ, $VST, $TLN, $CEG`

**Energy Power 5: My High Conviction Plays for 2026**

I did AI infrastructure last post because that’s where the capex tsunami started. Now energy because none of that AI buildout happens without massive new power generation. After filtering the noise, here’s my refined basket: **CEG, VST, CCJ, TLN, OKLO**. This lineup covers the full nuclear/AI power stack: fleet operators, diversified generators, uranium fuel, co-located data center plays, and next-gen SMR breakouts.

These are high beta bets on the AI power crunch: nuclear restarts, hyperscaler PPAs, uranium deficits, co-location campuses, and small modular reactors. Volatility will be there but the multi-year demand surge justifies the risk if you can handle the ride through 2026’s power capex cycle.

-----

The Picks (Deep Dive)

1. $CEG  ~$360

**Why I’m in:** Largest nuclear fleet in the US with unmatched scale. Microsoft deal to restart Three Mile Island Unit 1 exclusively for data center power locked in multi-decade revenue. Additional hyperscaler conversations ramping as tech giants scramble for carbon-free baseload. Earnings exploding from higher power prices and PPA re-contracting.

**Financials:** Market cap ~$114B, TTM revenue ~$25B+. Highly profitable with billions in net income and strong EBITDA growth on nuclear premium pricing and deals. Forward P/E ~22-25x range reflects execution visibility.

**The risks:** Regulatory hurdles on restarts, any safety headline risk hits the whole sector. High multiples leave little room for execution slips. Grid interconnection delays could push timelines.

**My take:** This is my anchor position. Proven operator with defensible moat in clean firm power. Heaviest weight here for long-term AI power share gains.

-----

2. $VST  ~$162

**Why I’m in:** Diversified mix of nuclear, gas, and renewables positioned perfectly for AI demand hotspots (Texas, Midwest). Multiple long-term PPAs signed with hyperscalers driving visible earnings growth. Already delivered massive upside in 2024-2025 on power price inflection - more to come as data centers cluster near their assets.

**Financials:** Market cap ~$55B, TTM revenue ~$17.2B, Net income ~$960M. EBITDA ~$5.2B with operating margins ~21%. Forward P/E ~17x—undemanding for the growth runway.

**The risks:** Natural gas exposure adds commodity volatility. Competitive wholesale markets can pressure margins if new supply floods in. Balance sheet leverage from growth capex.

**My take:** Proven winner that keeps executing. This is the growth engine of the basket with broad exposure to the power surge. Heavy allocation for multi-year runway.

-----

3. $CCJ  ~$93

**Why I’m in:** The premier uranium producer with tier-one assets and long-term contracts. Global uranium supply deficit widening as reactors extend lives and new builds come online. Hyperscaler nuclear commitments indirectly tighten the fuel market—CCJ captures pricing upside with sold-out production through 2026+.

**Financials:** Market cap ~$41B, TTM revenue ~$3.5B, Net income ~$526M. EBITDA ~$936M with profit margins ~15%. Forward P/E ~67x prices in multi-year uranium supercycle.

**The risks:** Uranium spot price cyclicality is present. Geopolitical risks in supply chain. Any demand slowdown from delayed reactor restarts.

**My take:** Highest conviction on the fuel supercycle despite volatility. Overweighting CCJ for asymmetric leverage to nuclear buildout. Heavy but sized for the swings.

-----

4. $TLN  ~$385

**Why I’m in:** Pure-play co-location story. Owns massive nuclear campuses with direct data center adjacency. AWS/Cumulus deal for dedicated power from Susquehanna plant proves the model. Pipeline of similar transactions as hyperscalers pay premiums for firm, on-site generation. Revenue ramping hard off low base.

**Financials:** Market cap ~$18B, TTM revenue ~$2.3B, Net income ~$226M. EBITDA ~$617M with high operating margins ~35%. Forward P/E ~20x on deal inflection.

**The risks:** Customer concentration heavy on a few deals. Post-restructuring volatility, execution risk on campus expansions. Premium valuation requires flawless delivery.

**My take:** Breakout play on the co-location trend. This is where AI power meets real estate which is massive if thesis scales. Medium allocation for upside but volatile.

-----

 5. $OKLO  ~$77

**Why I’m in:** Next-gen small modular reactor leader with fast-neutron tech designed for data center scale. Backed by Sam Altman, first commercial deployments targeted 2026-2027. Hyperscalers actively evaluating SMRs for behind-the-meter power—OKLO’s pipeline positions it as early winner.

**Financials:** Market cap ~$12B, effectively pre-revenue with TTM net losses ~$77M and negative EBITDA. Pure speculative valuation on SMR deployment potential—no traditional metrics apply yet.

**The risks:** Pre-revenue, regulatory approvals pending, first-of-kind execution risk huge. Dilution likely on capex ramp. Trading at moonshot multiples.

**My take:** Speculative lottery ticket on SMR monopoly. Smallest position but triple-digit potential if deployments hit. Not for the faint of heart.

-----

**Entry strategy:** Scaling in over 4-8 weeks to average into year-end volatility and expecting a dip in January / February. Using 10-15% dips to add to core positions (CEG/VST/CCJ).

### Post #60: 2025-12-29 19:57 — `$HUMA, $ORBS`

**Don’t Be Someone’s Exit Liquidity**

Seeing @squeezekid back pumping $ORBS again. Let me save you some money: **stop following degens shilling their plays.**

The Hard Truth About Trading Communities

When someone is posting non-stop about a ticker, ask yourself: **Why do they need YOU in this trade?**

If their thesis is so strong, why aren’t they quietly accumulating? Why are they begging for engagement and trying to recruit followers into their position?

Answer: Because they’re already in and need volume to exit profitably.

The DYOR Framework

Before touching ANY ticker someone is shilling:

**Ask These Questions:**

- Do they have a verified position? (Screenshot or ban)
- What’s their entry price vs current price? (Are they up 50% trying to recruit you at the top?)
- What’s the actual catalyst? (Not hopium, actual news/earnings/data)
- What’s the chart showing? (Is it parabolic? Breaking down? Consolidating?)
- What’s the risk/reward at CURRENT price? (Not where they got in)

**Do Your Own Research:**

- Pull up the chart on multiple timeframes
- Check recent news and earnings
- Look at volume and liquidity
- Calculate your own risk/reward
- Size position based on YOUR risk tolerance

**Red Flags:**

- Posts with 🚀🚀🚀 and zero substance
- “This is going to 10x trust me bro”
- Constant pumping without acknowledging risks
- No verified position shown
- They’re already up huge but “just getting started”
- Every post is about the same ticker

My $HUMA Mistake

I broke my own rules because I got caught up in the excitement. Someone I respected was posting about it. The community was bullish. I didn’t want to miss out.

So I jumped in without doing proper DD. Without checking the chart structure. Without having MY OWN thesis.

Result: -XX% loss and a lesson that cost me real money.

**The lesson wasn’t “don’t listen to others.”** It was “verify everything yourself before risking YOUR capital.”

The Right Way to Use Community DD

AfterHour is powerful BECAUSE of collective intelligence. But that doesn’t mean blindly following plays.

**Use the community smartly:**

- Get ticker ideas from others
- Read their DD as a starting point
- THEN do your own research
- THEN form your own thesis
- THEN decide if YOU want to take the trade
- THEN size it based on YOUR risk tolerance

Other people’s positions should give you ideas, not trade signals.

Bottom Line

Nobody cares about your money more than you do.

Not me. Not anyone posting DD.

If you lose money following someone else’s play, you have no one to blame but yourself. Because you chose to hit the buy button without doing the work.

**DYOR isn’t optional. It’s the price of entry to this game.**

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bdGM/dont-be-someones-exit-liquidit*

### Post #68: 2025-12-31 23:36 — `$SPY`

**2025 Is Over. Now What?**

Another year gone. Another year of wins, losses, lessons, and late nights staring at charts wondering if you made the right call.

I’m not going to sugarcoat it: trading in 2025 was rough for a lot of people due to Q1. Liberation day fucked a lot of people and it took months to regain positions. Some people ended up not beating $SPY and that’s ok.

But here’s what I learned this year, and why I’m more convicted than ever going into 2026:

**The market doesn’t care about your goals.**

It doesn’t care that you want to quit your job. It doesn’t care that you need to make $10K this month. It doesn’t care about your New Year’s resolutions or your vision board or your motivation.

The market rewards one thing: **discipline over time.**

Not genius. Not luck. Not following the hottest trader on social media.

Discipline. Executed consistently. Over months and years.

-----

What Actually Matters

I spent most of 2024 chasing. Chasing setups, chasing returns, chasing the next big win that would change everything.

2025 taught me to stop chasing and start building.

**Building a system.** Not a collection of random plays, but a repeatable process that works whether the market goes up, down, or sideways.

For me, that’s weekly premium collection with the wheel strategy. Nothing sexy. Nothing viral. But it works consistently.

That’s not luck. That’s a system.

**Building character.** Money amplifies who you already are. If you’re undisciplined with $10K, you’ll be undisciplined with $100K. The number just makes the consequences bigger.

Trading teaches you about yourself faster than anything else. Your patience. Your discipline. Your ability to take a loss and move on. Your ego when you’re right.

I’ve watched people blow up accounts not because they didn’t know how to trade, but because they couldn’t handle being wrong. They couldn’t take the small loss. They doubled down out of pride. They blamed the market instead of looking in the mirror.

**Building transparency.** When you know people are watching, you trade differently. You size positions correctly. You stick to your stops. You don’t YOLO into stupid plays because you’re bored.

That’s the power of verified positions.

-----

What I’m Leaving in 2025

**The myth that more is better.** More strategies. More indicators. More screens. More Discord servers.

You know what I learned? **One strategy, mastered, beats ten strategies half-assed.**

Pick your edge. Build your system. Execute it relentlessly.

**The fantasy that one trade changes everything.** We’ve all seen the screenshots. The guy who turned $5K into $500K on 0DTE SPY calls.

That’s not a strategy. That’s a lottery ticket with extra steps.

Real wealth isn’t built on one trade. It’s built on hundreds of small, disciplined decisions that compound over time.

**The belief that the market owes me anything.** It doesn’t.

Sometimes you do everything right and still lose. That’s not failure. That’s probability.

The only failure is not having a plan for when you’re wrong.

-----

What I’m Taking Into 2026

**Radical transparency.** Every position posted. Every week documented. Every lesson shared.

Because the community gets smarter together. Because verified positions build trust. Because showing losses makes the wins mean something.

**Unwavering discipline.** One system. Executed perfectly. No exceptions.

**Long-term thinking.** This isn’t about getting rich in 2026. It’s about building a foundation that works for decades.

Small consistent wins compounded over years. That’s when numbers get stupid.

But only if you don’t blow up. Only if you stay disciplined. Only if you think in decades, not days.

**Gratitude for this community.** AfterHour changed how I trade. Not because someone gave me a signal. But because seeing other people’s verified positions wins AND losses made me more honest with myself.

This is what trading should be. Not gurus. Not shills. Not fake screenshots.

Real people. Real positions. Real accountability.

-----

My Commitment to You

In 2026, I will:

- Keep my account linked and verified
- Link with Kinfo and provide portfolio updates for full transparency
- Document every lesson learned
- Admit when I’m wrong
- Build this community through transparency

I won’t promise you’ll make money following my plays. I won’t promise my system is perfect.

I promise I’ll show up. I promise I’ll be honest. I promise my positions will always be verified.

That’s all I can control.

-----

The Question That Matters

Here’s what you need to ask yourself as we head into 2026:

**Are you serious about this, or are you just entertained by it?**

Because if you’re serious, you need to:

- Pick ONE strategy and master it
- Track EVERY trade in a journal
- Build a SYSTEM, not a collection of random plays
- Think in YEARS, not weeks

If you’re just here for entertainment, that’s fine. The market is the best reality show on earth.

But don’t confuse entertainment with edge.

-----

Final Thought

2025 is over. You can’t change what happened.

You can only control what you do next.

You can keep doing what you’ve been doing Or you can get disciplined. Build a system. Execute it relentlessly. Share it transparently. Learn from losses. Compound small wins.

**The choice is yours.**

The market will be here tomorrow. And the day after. And every day for the rest of your life.

The question is: will you?

-----

See you in the markets.

Let’s make 2026 count and thank you for all the support so far since I joined this platform 🎄❄️🙏😊

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bdRr/2025-is-over-now-what*

### Post #71: 2026-01-03 16:43

**Silver’s Wild Ride: $130 Physical vs $72 Paper**

While the whole app talks about politics let’s talk about Silver

**MASSIVE arbitrage event unfolding right now:**

Physical silver trading at **$130/oz in Tokyo** while paper contracts sit at **$72/oz**. This is one of the largest disconnects in recorded history.

**Other physical markets:**

- Kuwait: $106/oz
- Korea: $97/oz

**What’s “paper” vs “physical”?**

**Paper contracts** = Futures/derivatives on exchanges like COMEX. You’re trading a contract representing silver, not actual metal. Cash-settled, leveraged, liquid.

**Physical** = Actual silver bars/coins you can hold. Real metal, real delivery, real storage costs.

Normally these prices track tight because of arbitrage. **Not anymore.**

**What’s causing this historic disconnect?**

**Margin requirement increases** are forcing retail traders to liquidate paper positions during thin holiday liquidity. Exchanges are raising the cash required to hold positions, forcing leveraged traders to sell.

We saw this playbook with platinum/palladium—now it’s silver’s turn.

The kicker: Current leverage on COMEX is **nowhere near 2011 peak levels** despite silver’s massive run. They’re squeezing retail out systematically.

**Why this matters:**

This shows the **paper market is illiquid and manipulated** during low-volume periods. Meanwhile physical demand is **screaming** while supply can’t keep up:

- Silver production is **20% below peak levels**
- Central bank currency wars intensifying
- Samsung battery tech + solar demand driving utility
- Retail traders now represent ~33% of market volume

**My take:**

This volatility is a feature, not a bug. **When everyone’s watching, the easy gains are gone**.

Paper contracts getting absolutely worked while physical holders sitting pretty. If you can’t take delivery, you’re trading at the mercy of margin clerks and exchange rules.

**Chart levels to watch (paper contracts):**

- $70-72/oz critical support zone
- Break below = more downside to $60s
- Hold here = potential bounce back to $80

The disconnect between physical and paper is **shenanigans at the highest level**. Banks, exchanges, and algos creating extreme moves during illiquid periods.

**2026 outlook:**

Expect extreme volatility to continue. We’re in a new era for precious metals after a decade+ of suppression. The question isn’t IF paper and physical converge it’s WHEN and at what price.

*Position: Long-term physical holder, not chasing paper here. This setup is too wild for short-term derivative plays.*

What’s your take—does this gap close by paper going up or physical coming down? 👇​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​

I wanted to expand on what he wrote and provide my evaluation for 2nd and 3rd order consequences. Now back to the post. Also tagging @mphinance as he is a G. 

———————————-

Following the capture of Nicolás Maduro on January 3, 2026, early indicators show “celebratory stability” rather than widespread civil unrest. With opposition leader María Corina Machado calling for calm and U.S. oversight established, the market is pivoting from “Crisis Hedge” to “Accelerated Reconstruction.”

The alpha is in the boring infrastructure rebuild.

Im talking about the the multi-year contracts required to resurrect a petrostate. This is the most institutional version of the trade yet, designed for maximum capex capture in the orderly transition scenario.

-----

TIER 1: The “Immediate Alpha” (Core Weighting)

**$VLO (Valero Energy)** – Refining Arbitrage. The #1 winner. $VLO’s Gulf Coast complexity is optimized for Venezuelan heavy-sour crude. They capture the highest margins from the new supply flood, regardless of global oil prices.

**$SLB (Schlumberger)** – Reservoir Intelligence. No drill bit turns without SLB’s reservoir maps. They are the essential “operating system” for the Orinoco Belt’s return to 2M+ bpd.

**$KBR** – The Reconstruction Proxy. The State Dept preferred provider for post-conflict logistics. They will manage base ops, energy facility security, and civil transition infrastructure.

**$COP (ConocoPhillips)** – The Asset Swap. A unique play on legal recovery. $10B+ in judgments will likely be converted into direct equity in prime oil blocks, turning a legal “dead asset” into a massive production engine.

**$PWR (Quanta Services)** – The Grid Stabilizer. Venezuela’s grid is at near-total failure. Quanta provides the high-voltage transmission crews necessary to power the pumps and ports.

-----

TIER 2: Production Muscle & Midstream (Volume Plays)

Getting the barrels out of the ground and to the Gulf Coast.

**$HAL (Halliburton)** – The “Muscle.” They lead in well-completions and workovers—the fastest way to bring dormant wells back online.

**$WFRD (Weatherford)** – The “Niche” winner. Specialized in the older, high-viscosity reservoirs common in the Orinoco. Deeply undervalued for this specific terrain.

**$FLR (Fluor)** – The EPC Giant. Only a handful of firms can rebuild the “Upgraders” (heavy oil chemical plants). $FLR is the likely lead for these multi-billion dollar contracts.

**$GLDD (Great Lakes Dredge & Dock)** – The Monopoly. Supertankers (VLCCs) can’t dock in silted-up Venezuelan ports. GLDD is the only U.S. firm capable of the emergency dredging required for export.

**$EPD (Enterprise Products)** – The “Toll Booth.” Their Gulf Coast terminals and blending facilities will be the primary destination for the “New Heavy” volume.

-----

TIER 3: The “Modernization” Layer (3rd Order Growth)

**$J (Jacobs Solutions)** – The Architect. Swap-in for front-end engineering. They win the “Stage 1” planning contracts for the country’s new water and energy master plans.

**$ET (Energy Transfer)** – The Volume Aggregator. Their expansive crude network is perfectly positioned to move Venezuelan barrels from the ports to the heart of the U.S. refining complex.

**$MTRX (Matrix Service)** – The Tank specialist. Essential for repairing the decayed storage tanks that currently prevent safe oil blending and export.

**$PLTR (Palantir)** – The Governance OS. Essential for auditing PDVSA, tracking aid, and securing the digital perimeter of a new government.

**$NU (NuBank)** – The FinTech Disruptor. Captures 30M unbanked Venezuelans via digital-first banking in a newly stabilized, dollarized economy.

-----

### Post #76: 2026-01-06 02:24 — `$ASTS, $IREN, $ONDS, $GLXY`

**🚀 Parabolic Moves & The Discipline Trap**

The “Community Favorites” are absolutely **flying** today.

$IREN +12.95%  
$ONDS +13.61%  
$GLXY  +6.23%  
$ASTS +8.88%  

When you see double-digit gains in a single session, the FOMO isn’t just a feeling it’s a **physical itch** to click “Buy.”

**This is exactly where traders separate themselves from gamblers.**

-----

1. The Art of the “Exit” (Short Positions)

If you’re in a short-term trade and your target was hit today—**take the win**.

- A 13% move in a day is a **gift from the market**
- Don’t let a “good trade” turn into a “bad hold” because you got greedy for an extra 2%
- Lock in that profit and move your stop losses up on the rest

-----

2. The Power of “Doing Nothing” (Long Positions)

For those in long-term conviction plays today is a **test of temperament**.

- If you’ve held through the lows, don’t feel pressured to add more just because it’s “moving”
- Sometimes the most profitable move is to **watch the screen and do absolutely nothing**
- Your position is already working for you

-----

3. Stop Chasing the Highs

Chasing a stock that is already up 10%+ on the day is a recipe for getting trapped at the local top.

**Wait for the Consolidation**: No stock goes up in a straight line forever.

**Look Elsewhere**: If  one sector is overextended, look for sectors that haven’t moved yet. There’s always a high-quality entry point somewhere else.

-----

 **Professional Reminder**: You don’t need to catch every move to be a successful trader. You just need to catch the ones that fit your setup.

Let the FOMO pass…the market will be open again tomorrow.

**Stay disciplined. Protect your capital.**

**Greed Is Just Getting Started – It can go higher 🚀💰**

Fear and Greed Index: 51 (Neutral)

If you’re watching small caps rip and community favorites pump this week, you might think we’re at peak greed. Well ladies and gentlemen I think this is not the end.

A Fear & Greed reading of 51 means one thing: the broad market hasn’t even started chasing this move yet. We’re likely in the “Small-Cap Catch-Up” phase—the early innings of FOMO, not the final chapter.

**The Small-Cap Rotation Is Real**

When I refer to “small caps,” I’m specifically looking at the engine of the domestic economy: companies typically valued under $2 billion. These stocks have reached nearly 100-year lows in relative market cap compared to large caps. What we’re seeing now appears to be a massive rotation highlighted by the Russell 2000 hitting fresh highs just this week.

People are asking: “Is this the top? Should I take profits?”

Wrong question. The right question is: “When does this rotation turn into destructive euphoria?”

**What The Research Tells Us About “Neutral” Readings**

- The Small-Cap Engine is Hot: Components like Junk Bond Demand (spreads at a razor-thin 2.76%) and Stock Price Strength (new 52-week highs) are screaming "Risk-On." Because small caps rely on cheap credit and broad participation, these are the leading indicators for our move. Research shows an 84-92% correlation between these tight spreads and small-cap outperformance.

- The Large-Cap Anchor is Cold: The Market Momentum component is tied exclusively to the S&P 500. Since the “Magnificent 7” and most big tech have been "chilling" (aside from names like Google and Amazon), this keeps the broad index reading as "Neutral."

- Sentiment is Balanced: The Put/Call Ratio is around 0.70. In a true market top, you usually see this collapse to lows below 0.5 as everyone gambles on calls. We are seeing healthy skepticism which is exactly what rallies need to climb higher.

**Here’s What Neutral Greed Could Mean: Permission to Run**

Quantitative backtests suggest that the “meat of the move” often happens between 50 and 70 on the sentiment scale:

**Extreme Fear (<25):** High return potential, but high risk of “falling knives.”

**Neutral (50-60):** The potential “Sweet Spot.” This is where a trend is confirmed but not yet exhausted. Research shows that when F&G crosses above 50 from below, it often acts as a “permission” signal. The highest win rates for small-cap longs tend to occur in the 50-65 range, not at the extremes.

**Peak Greed (>75):** This is where the data suggests you tighten stops and prepare for potential snap-back.

**The Path Forward: Likely Grind Higher With Bumps**

The typical pattern from neutral readings suggests a grind toward greed territory over the coming weeks. Small pullbacks are a feature, not a bug, of a healthy rotation. These dips often shake out weak hands while allowing the broader trend to continue building.

**Coming Next: The 9,000 Trade Backtest**

In my next post, I’ll share the data from a study of 9,000+ trades taken exclusively by using the Fear & Greed Index for entries and exits. The findings were super interesting. 

**Bottom line:** Greed appears to be just starting to build. Small caps are leading the way, and the broad index is just starting to wake up. Jumping ship now could mean missing out on the most profitable part of the cycle.

### Post #78: 2026-01-10 15:24

**Understanding the Fear & Greed Index: A Deep Dive for Traders 📈📉**

I realized from my last post a lot of the newer traders don’t fully understand the components that make up the fear and greed index and how to use it so let’s  actually understand what’s *under the hood* of this widely-cited metric?

Let’s break down exactly how this index works, what it’s measuring, and most importantly how you can use it intelligently in your trading strategy.

-----

What Is the CNN Fear & Greed Index?

Launched in 2011, the CNN Fear & Greed Index attempts to quantify the dominant emotion driving the stock market at any given moment. The premise is simple but powerful: **markets are driven by two primary emotions—fear and greed.**

- **Fear** drives investors to sell, pushing prices down
- **Greed** drives investors to buy, pushing prices up

The index aggregates **seven different market indicators** into a single score ranging from **0 (Extreme Fear) to 100 (Extreme Greed)**. Each component gets equal weighting, and the average produces the final reading you see.

**The Scale:**

- 0-24: Extreme Fear
- 25-44: Fear
- 45-55: Neutral
- 56-75: Greed
- 76-100: Extreme Greed

-----

The Seven Components: What’s Actually Being Measured

1. **Market Momentum (S&P 500 vs. 125-Day MA)**

**What it measures:** How far the S&P 500 is trading above or below its 125-day moving average.

**Why it matters:** This captures trend strength. When the market is well above its moving average, momentum is strong (greed). When it’s below, momentum has stalled or reversed (fear).

**Trading insight:** The 125-day MA (~6 months) is a medium-term trend indicator. Large deviations signal potential exhaustion or reversal zones. If you trade mean reversion, extreme readings here can flag overbought/oversold conditions on a macro scale.

-----

2. **Stock Price Strength (52-Week Highs vs. Lows)**

**What it measures:** The number of stocks hitting 52-week highs vs. 52-week lows on the NYSE.

**Why it matters:** This shows market breadth at the individual stock level. In a healthy bull market, you’ll see many more highs than lows. When fear dominates, the ratio flips.

**Trading insight:** This is a **breadth indicator**. Even if the S&P 500 is making new highs, if only a handful of mega-cap stocks are driving it (narrow breadth), the foundation is weak. Conversely, seeing broad participation in a rally (many stocks at 52-week highs) suggests sustainable strength. Use this to confirm or question the headline index moves.

-----

3. **Stock Price Breadth (Advancing Volume vs. Declining Volume)**

**What it measures:** The volume of shares traded in advancing stocks vs. declining stocks on the NYSE.

**Why it matters:** Volume validates price movement. High advancing volume shows conviction behind buying. High declining volume shows conviction behind selling.

**Trading insight:** This is another **breadth metric**, but volume-weighted. You can have price strength without volume (weak rally) or volume without price strength (distribution). When both align - high advancing volume with rising prices then that’s **confirmation**. Watch for divergences: market up but declining volume higher = red flag.

-----

4. **Put/Call Ratio (Options Sentiment)**

**What it measures:** The ratio of put options purchased vs. call options purchased.

**Why it matters:** Options traders vote with their wallets. Heavy put buying (hedging or betting on downside) signals fear. Heavy call buying signals greed and bullish positioning.

**Trading insight:** This is a **contrarian indicator** at extremes. When *everyone* is buying puts (high put/call ratio), it often marks a bottom because fear is maxed out. When everyone’s loading calls (low put/call ratio), tops can form. 

**Example:** In March 2020 (COVID crash), put/call ratios spiked to historic levels. That marked the bottom within weeks as panic peaked.

-----

5. **Market Volatility (VIX on a 50-Day MA)**

**What it measures:** The CBOE Volatility Index (VIX) the market’s “fear gauge” smoothed with a 50-day moving average.

**Why it matters:** VIX measures expected 30-day volatility based on S&P 500 options pricing. High VIX = high uncertainty and fear. Low VIX = complacency and greed.

**Trading insight:** The 50-day MA smooths out daily noise. VIX below 15 historically signals complacency (greed), while VIX above 30 signals panic (fear). **VIX spikes don’t last** they mean-revert. 

**Pro tip:** VIX and the S&P 500 are inversely correlated ~80% of the time. When that correlation breaks (both rising or both falling), pay attention something unusual is happening in market structure.

-----

6. **Junk Bond Demand (High-Yield Spread)**

**What it measures:** The yield spread between junk bonds (high-yield corporate debt) and investment-grade bonds.

**Why it matters:** When investors are greedy, they chase yield and pile into riskier junk bonds, compressing the spread. When fear rises, they flee to safety (investment-grade), widening the spread.

**Trading insight:** This measures **risk appetite in credit markets**. Credit markets often lead equities. If junk bond spreads are widening (fear in credit), equity volatility usually follows. This is an early warning system. 
-----

 7. **Safe Haven Demand (Stocks vs. Treasuries)**

**What it measures:** The performance difference between stocks (S&P 500) and U.S. Treasury bonds over the past 20 trading days.

**Why it matters:** When fear rises, money flows into “safe haven” assets like Treasuries, and stocks underperform. When greed dominates, money flows out of bonds and into equities for higher returns.

**Trading insight:** This captures **flow dynamics** between risk-on (stocks) and risk-off (bonds) positioning. If stocks are outperforming Treasuries, risk appetite is strong. If Treasuries are outperforming, institutions are de-risking.

-----

How to Actually Use This Index in Your Trading

Now that you understand the mechanics, let’s talk application. The Fear & Greed Index is **not a timing signal** it’s a **context indicator**. Here’s how you can use it:

1. **Contrarian Entries at Extremes**

The index works best at **extremes** (below 25 or above 75). Markets can stay overbought longer than you can stay solvent, but extreme fear/greed readings often precede reversals.

**Extreme Fear (0-25):** Market is panicking. This is where you start looking for **dip-buying opportunities** on quality setups for the long term - at this point we usually crashed.

**Extreme Greed (75-100):** Market is euphoric. This is where you **tighten stops**, **take profits**, and **reduce position sizes**. At this point we are usually near a top 

2. **Confirming Market Context**

- **High Fear (20-40):** Bias toward long setups, dip buying, cash-secured puts on quality names
- **Neutral (40-60):** Trade the technicals, no strong bias
- **High Greed (60-80):** Bias toward taking profits, protective puts, reducing exposure

3. **Divergence Hunting**

Watch for **divergences** between the index and price action:

- **Market making new highs but Fear & Greed not at extremes:** Suggests the rally has room to run (healthy)
- **Market flat but Fear & Greed at extremes:** Signals tension—watch for a breakout or breakdown
- **Index at Extreme Greed but market stalling:** Potential top forming (distribution)
-----

What the Index Does NOT Tell You

Let’s be clear on limitations:

 **It’s not a timing tool.** Markets can stay fearful or greedy for extended periods. Don’t short just because greed is high or buy just because fear is high without technical confirmation.

**It’s backward-looking.** All seven components measure past data. The index tells you *what happened*, not *what will happen*.

**Equal weighting has flaws.** Not all components are equally relevant in every market environment. During a credit crisis, junk bond spreads matter more. During a vol spike, VIX matters more. The index treats them all the same.

 **It doesn’t predict Black Swans.** The index was at neutral levels in February 2020 before COVID crashed markets. Unpredictable events override sentiment indicators.

-----

Resources

**CNN Fear & Greed Index:** 
https://www.cnn.com/markets/fear-and-greed

-----

Final Thoughts

The CNN Fear & Greed Index is a **powerful macro sentiment tool** when used correctly. It won’t tell you when to enter or exit specific trades, but it will tell you **what kind of market environment you’re in**.

**What’s your experience with the Fear & Greed Index? Drop your thoughts below. 👇**

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/beLx/understanding-the-fear-and-gre*

### Post #80: 2026-01-11 16:26

**I bet most of you did not know this 😉**

This week January 12th - 16th we have the BCOM Rebalance happening which is a real, annual occurrence in the financial world that often causes “phantom” price moves which is basically volatility driven by rules rather than news. This literally happens every single year at this time. 

What is the BCOM Rebalance?

The Bloomberg Commodity Index (BCOM) is one of the world’s most influential benchmarks for “hard assets.” It tracks the prices of 20+ different commodities, from Gold and Crude Oil to Corn and Lean Hogs.

Because billions of dollars in “passive” funds (like ETFs) are designed to mirror this index exactly, they must buy and sell whatever the index tells them to. Every January, the index resets its weights to ensure it remains diversified.

The “15% Rule”

No single commodity can exceed 15% of the total index.

- Throughout 2025, Gold and Silver had a massive rally, causing their “weight” in the index to balloon.

- By the end of the year, Gold reached roughly 20.4% of the index.

- The Result: Between January 12 and January 16, 2026, these funds are legally required to sell off their “extra” Gold to bring it back down to the 15% limit.

Why are you mentioning this?

In 2026, this rebalance is expected to be more “violent” because the performance gap between metals and other commodities (like energy) was so large last year.

The “Forced” Selling Pressure

Analysts estimate that roughly $109 billion follows this index. Because Gold and Silver performed so well, the rebalance is triggering a “mechanical” sell-off:

- Gold: Estimated $7 billion in selling pressure.
- Silver: Estimated $7.1 billion in selling pressure (dropping from ~9.6% to 3.9%).

- The Impact: This can cause Gold and Silver prices to dip temporarily, even if there is no “bad news” in the world.

The “Forced” Buying (The Winners)

The money sold from metals has to go somewhere. In 2026, the primary beneficiaries are:

- Cocoa: Re-entering the index this year, sparking a wave of “forced buying.”
- Energy: WTI and Brent Crude Oil are seeing weight increases, providing a technical “tailwind” for prices.

For the new folks joining the community

Just because we may get a small drop don’t mean the “bull market is over”. If you wanted to buy leaps for gold and silver use this week as your chance.

Summary Table: 2026 Rebalance Impact


Gold  📉 Selling - Exceeded the 15% cap after a 2025 rally.        

Silver 📉 Selling - Significant reduction in target weight for 2026.      

Cocoa  📈 Buying - Being added back to the index after a long absence.   

Crude Oil 📈 Buying - Target weights increased to balance the energy sector.


Best of luck to you all for this upcoming week and have a blessed Sunday! o

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/beOc/i-bet-most-of-you-did-not-know*

**Let’s talk Dark Pool transactions and the future of this market. Deep Dive 👇👇👇**

Massive dark pool transactions are hitting MAGS (Roundhill Magnificent 7 ETF), Bitcoin, and Silver at critical technical levels. Breadth improving across the board but defense rotation accelerating hard. Tech P/E compressing as market questions AI returns. International markets breaking out while everyone watches MAG7 stagnate. CPI tomorrow could spark serious vol.

The Unusual Activity That Caught My Eye

We just witnessed the **largest dark pool transaction ever recorded** on MAGS (Magnificent 7 ETF) - and get this, it happened at almost the exact same price level as 6 months ago. MAG7 has basically done nothing for half a year while small caps, energy, and commodities have been absolutely ripping.

Here’s what jumps out at me: This looks like a distribution pattern forming. Wall Street is repositioning, and when you see the #1 all-time dark pool transaction at a 6-month resistance level, someone definitely knows something.

The Silver Squeeze Is Actually Real

**SIVR (physical silver ETF)** just recorded a dark pool sweep - the second major transaction in the recent 2 weeks.  But here’s where it gets interesting: Silver is trading in **positive gamma squeeze territory** right now, well above key $72 strikes on SLV, currently pushing toward $80.

The paper market is showing serious illiquidity. Someone’s getting squeezed hard, and it’s creating absolutely wild volatility. We’re seeing massive transactions in precious metals across the board - gold, silver, and probably platinum and palladium next.

**Short-term?** This is dangerous and overextended. **Long-term?** Still bullish on metals, but I’d expect consolidation after this vertical move before the next leg up.

Bitcoin’s Mystery Transaction

Largest dark pool sweep ever on **BITB** - and it was a *fast* transaction (sweep = urgency, someone needed to move quickly). But look at price action - still range-bound between $86K support and $94.5K resistance.

Four straight days of ETF outflows after two strong days of inflows to start the year (classic window dressing then rebuying). Yet a whale just made a massive move in the middle of the range. This screams accumulation while retail capitulates.

**The breakout level to watch: $94.5K**. Below $86K and we’ve got real problems. The liquidation levels are crystal clear on both sides.

Ethereum’s Hidden Strength

While everyone’s obsessing over Bitcoin, **Ethereum just hit all-time high transaction volume** - 2.2M transactions per day, up **62.4% year-over-year**.

This isn’t just crypto hype - this is actual infrastructure adoption happening in real-time. The blockchain is processing more activity than ever, which typically comes before major price moves. Don’t sleep on ETH while Bitcoin gets all the headlines.

The Defense Rotation Nobody’s Talking About

**$XLP (Consumer Staples)** just broke out of a textbook inverse head-and-shoulders pattern - right as Trump announced $200B flowing into Freddie Mac and Fannie Mae.

Here’s what’s absolutely wild: The price action **front-ran the announcement by days**. Same exact thing happened with defense stocks - they were already running on Monday before the $500B+ defense spending announcement hit.

Staples, healthcare, and defense have all been rotating higher while MAG7 just sits there. **XLP vs SPY** shows we’re stagnating in staples relative strength for the first time in a while. Healthcare showing similar improvement.

**What this tells me**: Market’s turning defensive. Not bearish, but defensive. That’s a massive shift from 2024’s AI-everything rally. High beta vs low volatility, cyclicals vs defensives - all still making new highs, but the character is completely changing.

The Breadth Divergence You Need To Understand

**67.6% of S&P stocks** are now above their 200-day MA - first time in 3 months. This is exactly the type of breadth improvement that historically leads to further strength and dip-buying opportunities.

**NASDAQ’s advance-decline line** is also skyrocketing. This is what we call a bull market, not just a MAG7-driven rally anymore. When breadth expands like this, it’s usually sustainable.

**MAGS vs SPY** is forming a head-and-shoulders pattern to the downside. The ratio has been declining while the market makes new highs. That’s a real problem when 37-38% of the S&P is made up of these stocks alone.

**Small caps have been leading for months.** IWD vs IWF shows more defensive value outperformance. When small caps lead for 6+ months, it’s not something to celebrate - it’s an early recession warning (12-18 months out). I’m not worried about the next 6-9 months, but this absolutely matters for late 2026 and beyond.

The Semiconductor Reality Check

Despite NVDA being boring and stuck in a double bottom base pattern, **SMH (semiconductors)** just keeps grinding higher.

This is absolutely critical: **Modern Dow Theory** says if semis are making new highs, you don’t want to be bearish. Original Dow Theory (transports + industrials) both making ATHs, check. Modern version (semis + MAG7), check - even if MAG7 is weakening relatively.

**As long as semis are strong, don’t fight this market.** When semis give up, when MAG7 gives up - that’s when we can start talking about the market turning truly defensive.

NVDA needs to close above **$195** to bring in positive gamma and potentially break out of this range. Until then, it’s just a waiting game.

Tech’s Dirty Secret: P/E Compression

Here’s something nobody’s talking about: **Technology forward P/E is actually back to its 5-year mean**. Everyone thinks tech is getting more expensive, but earnings have been improving right alongside price.

**What’s really happening**: The market is a forward-looking mechanism (usually 18 months out). It’s starting to question what AI actually means for revenue generation at these companies pumping billions into data centers every single day.

This is P/E compression from valuation concern, not expansion from irrational exuberance. The market is repricing the AI narrative in real-time, and most people haven’t noticed yet.

Energy Sector Finally Waking Up

**Oil just hit nearly $60/barrel**, approaching the $60-61.50 level I’ve been watching for months. If we break through, this completely changes the downtrend narrative that everyone’s been so bearish on.

**XLE (Energy Select Sector)** is showing real signs of life after a brutal downtrend. This matters for two big reasons:

1. **Oil is a massive component of inflation**. If crude spikes, that eerily similar 1970s inflation chart becomes very real very fast.
1. **Oil usually shows stagflation first**. If we’re heading into a stagflationary environment (weak growth + persistent inflation), energy wakes up before anything else does.

The International Breakout I don’t see many people talking about 

While US investors are glued to MAG7, **international markets are breaking out to all-time highs**:

**China** - FXI skyrocketing with liquidity returning. Stocks like BILI (Bilibili) already running hard. This rotation started early and it’s accelerating fast.

**Germany (DAX)** - Broke out two weeks ago. Often the go-to defensive play in Europe, and it’s confirming global risk-on.

**Emerging Markets** - Multiple markets hitting ATHs while everyone’s focused domestically.

This is a huge tell. When international markets lead while US large caps stagnate, it’s a rotation into value, into growth outside the US, into markets that haven’t had their run yet.

Copper & Industrial Metals: The Quiet Strength

**Copper is holding up way better than expected**, and rare earth minerals, aluminum - basically the entire industrial metals complex looks really strong on longer-term charts.

This ties directly into the China story, infrastructure spending, and the global rotation theme. When copper’s strong, it’s usually signaling economic activity someone isn’t seeing yet in the data.

Gold’s Relentless March

**Gold just made a new all-time high** before pulling back slightly. Like silver, it’s overextended short-term but the trend is absolutely undeniable.

Let me be crystal clear: **This is not normal behavior.**

Implied volatility on silver is at **85 IV** - that’s 5-7% daily expected moves. These levels don’t last. Every time IV spikes like this, it mean reverts hard.

But here’s the truly terrifying part:

The Chart That Should Make You Very Careful

I pulled the **silver to gold ratio yearly rate of change** going back to 1969 (as far as the data goes).

**We just hit a record level. We’ve NEVER been this high before. Ever.**

Now look at what was happening every other time silver outperformed gold at extreme levels:

- **2020**: Pandemic
- **2011**: Operation Twist, everyone thought we were heading back into the Great Financial Crisis
- **1998**: Dot-com bubble building, but more importantly - **Long-Term Capital Management blew up**
- **1987**: Black Monday crash followed shortly after
- **1979**: Volcker era, inflation crisis, oil situation
- **1974**: Brutal 1973-74 bear market, multi-year lows in SPY

Every single time silver has spiked relative to gold like this, **something major was breaking in the markets.**

We Just Flipped Into Negative Gamma

Today we crossed underneath the gamma flip line. **We’re now in negative gamma territory** (or at minimum neutral-to-negative).

**What this means:**

- Volatility picks up
- Liquidity dries up
- Dealers stop buying dips and selling rips
- Markets get more correlated and choppy
- Sell-side can get very sell-side, buy-side can get very buy-side

### Post #83: 2026-01-17 15:37

**To my New Traders….. Comparison is the Thief of Conviction**

This year has been off to a great start for many of us. But maybe not for everyone. That is why I want to make a post and maybe for some of you this will sit close in your heart. 

My question to you is: are you currently seeing people post +20% in 2 weeks while you’re grinding smaller gains? 

That’s perfectly fine. Stay in your lane. FOMO kills more accounts than bad entries. 

The Scroll of Death

You open AfterHour. First post: guy up 28% YTD. Second post: 19% in two weeks. Third post: someone turned $10K into $20K on a single options play. Someone I saw was up 288% YTD and even I was like damn that’s crazy what am I doing 😂

You look at your account. Up 3% for the year. Maybe flat this week. Suddenly your disciplined approach feels like you’re moving in slow motion.

Here’s what that feeling is about to make you do: abandon the strategy you are currently trying to learn to chase what’s working for someone else right now or even just throw money into a play with no knowledge about the stock. 

What You Don’t See

I see a lot of those posts coming from people with reputation and mastery in the market.  These people are survivors of the market. They are someone who generally worked hard to get to where they are today. 

**What you don’t see:**

- The years of screen time that taught them how that stock moves
- The blown accounts before they figured out position sizing
- The months of research into that company’s fundamentals
- The strategy modifications after repeated failures
- The losses along the way that built their conviction
- The dozens of similar setups they passed on because something was off

**Their conviction didn’t come from free bullshit alerts.** It came from putting in the work.

You’re seeing the result of homework you didn’t do. Experience you haven’t earned yet. Pattern recognition you haven’t built.

And that’s fine. That’s exactly where you should be if you’re getting started.

High Conviction Takes Time to Build

Those big position sizes you see? That confidence to go heavy on a single play? **That comes from deep conviction.** And deep conviction comes from experience.

- They’ve traded it before and know its patterns
- They understand the company’s business model inside out
- They’ve studied the sector and macro factors

**You can’t copy that conviction.** You can copy the ticker and the entry price, but you can’t copy the years of context that make them confident putting real size on it.

If You’re Just Getting Started

**It’s okay to be learning.**

It’s okay that your positions are smaller. It’s okay that you’re not swinging for home runs yet. It’s okay that you’re feeling lost at times. 

This is the learning phase. This is where you:

- Figure out what setups you can actually recognize in real-time
- Learn what risk you can stomach without losing sleep
- Discover which strategies fit your personality
- Build the screen time to develop pattern recognition
- Take small losses that teach you instead of big losses that wreck you

Every trader posting massive gains went through this phase. 

Stop measuring yourself against other people’s P&L.

**Set a goal that makes sense for YOUR situation:**

If you’ve got a $5K account and you’re learning, maybe your goal is to not blow up for 3 months. Maybe it’s to hit 5% monthly while learning your setups. Maybe it’s to execute your stop losses without hesitation.

If you’ve got more capital but you’re still developing your edge, maybe your goal is consistency over big wins. Maybe it’s to master one specific strategy before adding others. Maybe it’s to build a watchlist of 5 stocks you know inside and out.

**Your goal should be based on where YOU are in the journey.** Not where someone else is.

Small Positions Are Smart Positions

If you’re taking smaller positions while you learn, you’re doing it right.

**Position size should match conviction.** And conviction should match experience.

If you’ve watched a stock for 2 weeks, your position should be small. If you’ve traded it 50 times over 6 months, your position can be bigger.

**You’ll get there.** But you get there by staying in the game long enough to build that conviction yourself.

Bottom Line

If you’re grinding smaller gains while everyone posts big numbers, **you’re probably exactly where you should be.**

Sustainable trading isn’t about who gains fastest in January. It’s about who’s still here in December. Still executing their system. Still building their account. Still learning and refining.

Those big gain posts? Respect them. Study them. Learn from them. But don’t let them steal your conviction in your own process.

**Set your own goals. Build your own conviction. Trust your own timeline.**

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bfrU/to-my-new-traders-comparison-i*

### Post #84: 2026-01-18 16:01

**🏛️ The $38 Trillion Question: Is the “Plumbing” Failing?**

This post was inspired by something @TerrifiedOfAI messaged me and he believes I am a good candidate to explain the topic so….. let’s hope I did not fuck this up. Now let’s talk Debt. 

Today the headlines are dominated by Trump and the storm he has in his head for the vision of America. But for the markets, the real story is the “plumbing” aka the flow of foreign capital that keeps the U.S. debt machine running. This is a very interesting topic to be discussing and upon doing research I learned some new things as well. 


- Quick Glossary for Key Terms

**Stock vs. Flow**: *Stock* is the total debt held. *Flow* is the new debt being issued. Because the U.S. now issues over **$8 billion in new debt every day**, the *flow* is what determines if interest rates spike or stay stable.

**Indirect Bidders**: A proxy for foreign demand in auctions, including central banks and international asset managers.

**Repo Facility (SRF)**: The “emergency valve” where banks swap Treasuries for cash. High usage means the private market has run out of spare liquidity.

-----

- The Anatomy of Foreign Ownership (The “Stock”)

As of **January 7, 2026**, the total gross national debt hit a staggering **$38.43 trillion**. While foreign holdings are at record dollar amounts, their *percentage* share of our total debt has been diluted by the massive surge in domestic issuance.

**Key Statistics (January 2026)**

**Foreign-Held Debt**
• Percentage of Total: ~24.3%
• Amount: Approximately $9.35 Trillion

**Share of Publicly Held Debt**
• Percentage: ~30.5%
• Included in foreign holdings above

**Top Foreign Holders**
• Japan: Approximately $1.20 Trillion
• UK: Approximately $888.5 Billion
• China: Approximately $682.6 Billion (17-year low)

-----

- The Auction “Front Door” (The “Flow”)

The paradox is simple: foreigners only hold 24.3% of our debt, yet they dominate the **new auctions**. While the 10-year average is 64%, recent instability has pushed reliance to extremes. In late 2025, Indirect Bidders soaked up over 80% of specific 10-year auctions, leaving primary dealers with record-low allocations.

**10-Year Average of New Issue Awards**

**2000s Era**
• Average Foreign Auction Share: ~55%
• Key Driver: Post-9/11 dollar strength

**2010s Era**
• Average Foreign Auction Share: ~60%
• Key Driver: Quantitative Easing (QE) and low yields

**2020s (to date)**
• Average Foreign Auction Share: **64%**
• Key Driver: **High U.S. yields attracting global capital**

**The “Flipping” Factor:** Why is auction demand so much higher than total holdings? Foreign hedge funds often buy at auction to capture the “new issue premium” and then immediately sell to U.S. pension funds. This means foreigners provide the **critical liquidity** at the moment the debt is born.

**Domestic Dynamics:**  U.S. banks are facing a “Liquidity Trap.” While some leverage rules have been softened to help them intermediate the market, the sheer volume of debt from the **OBBBA** is “crowding out” their ability to buy more. Foreigners are no longer just *preferred* buyers; they are **mathematically necessary**.

-----

- Why We Are Getting To a Turning Point

Three shocks are hitting the “64% safety net” simultaneously:

**The Powell Subpoenas:** Powell has characterized the DOJ’s criminal investigation as a “pretext” to pressure the Fed for rate cuts. For foreign central banks, this signals the end of Fed independence which would end up destroying the “Safe Haven” status of the dollar.

**The China-Gold Divergence:** While the UK scales up, **China has cut U.S. holdings for 14 straight months**, shifting reserves into **Gold**. This “Great Diversification” reduces the pool of reliable buyers.

**The Repo Squeeze:** Usage of the Standing Repo Facility spiked to **$74.6 billion on New Year’s Eve (Dec 31)**. While year-end spikes are normal, this volume confirms that despite $38T in debt, there is a shortage of usable cash in the banking system. If foreign demand drops, domestic banks have no cash left to fill the void.

-----

- The “Yield Shock” Scenario: If Demand Drops 10%

If a “legal indictment” of Powell or a “geopolitical shift” causes a 10% drop in foreign participation (from 64% to 54%), the fallout is quantifiable:

**30-Year Mortgages**
• Impact: 1-1.5% jump in rates
• Why It Matters: Pegged to 10-Year yields; instantly freezes housing market

**Nasdaq 100**
• Impact: 10-20% correction potential
• Why It Matters: Valuation compression on high-multiple growth stocks

**U.S. Budget**
• Impact: +$400B annual interest expense
• Why It Matters: Higher yields on $38T debt = faster deficit growth

-----

- The Bottom Line

Watch the **February 11, 2026, 10-year note auction**. If the “Indirect Bidder” share falls toward the **54%** handle, the “Yield Shock” will move from a theoretical risk to a market-wide reality.

**Monitoring Tips:**

**TIC Data:** Watch the February 18 release for “Long-Term Flow”

**SOFR Spikes:** If the Secured Overnight Financing Rate (SOFR) exceeds the Fed's target range (likely 3.25-3.50% in your scenario), it indicates that despite the "ample reserves" framework, banks are hoarding cash and the plumbing is clogged.

**Gold/USD Cross:** Historically, Gold and the Dollar trade inversely. If they rise together, it indicates extreme systemic fear. Investors are fleeing all paper assets (stocks/bonds) for safety (Gold) and liquidity (Cash/USD). This happened during the 2008 crash and is a classic "crisis correlation."

### Post #87: 2026-01-21 03:45

**Market Rotation Intensifying - Here’s What I’m Seeing**

Mega caps dropped 3%+ today while defensive sectors led - classic late-cycle behavior. Consumer staples showing relative strength against SPY at new highs is the same divergence pattern we saw before the 2021 correction and leading into 2007. We broke outside weekly implied moves on SPY, QQQ, and most of the Mag 7. AAPL hit a 3-standard deviation move. We’re in negative gamma now.

6 Days ago I gave you this article and warned you. People bullshit bear post for months and eventually get it right…. I got it right within a week just saying

https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bfcv/something-is-very-wrong-in-the

- The Pattern That Keeps Repeating

I’ve been tracking something that caught my attention last week, and today confirmed it. Consumer staples are putting in a higher low relative to SPY while the index itself is making higher highs.

This matters because I saw the exact inverse play out in April 2025 during the tariff selloff. Back then, staples showed relative strength while SPY was actually putting in a higher low - that marked the bottom perfectly. Now we’re seeing it flip the other way.

When SPY pushed to recent highs, consumer staples didn’t weaken. They held relative strength. Since that divergence started forming, the Mag 7 just handed us a 3%+ down day.

I went back and checked the history on this. In 2007, the S&P Retailing Industry Group made higher highs for over a year while the relative strength line made lower highs. That led straight into the financial crisis. Same setup in 2021 - retail making higher highs, relative strength declining, followed by a sharp correction.

Look, I’m not calling a crash. But year-long divergences don’t resolve quietly.

- Year-to-Date Performance Is Telling

Energy +6%. Materials +6%. Consumer Staples +6%. Industrials +5%.

These are your late-cycle sectors. When you see energy, materials, staples, and industrials outperforming growth on a relative basis, you’re typically near at least a temporary market top. It does not have to be a multi-year top, but the rotation is clear - money is moving out of growth and into safety.

- Today’s Action Breakdown

The damage in mega caps was real:

- AAPL pushed outside a 2-standard deviation daily move, briefly touching 3SD before recovering into the close
- NVDA and AVGO both got hit hard
- TSLA and AMZN both down over 3%

Meanwhile consumer staples, energy, healthcare, and utilities led the market. Pure risk-off.

- We’re Outside Weekly Ranges Now

SPY broke through the lower weekly implied move around $680. We got the break. This is what negative gamma looks like - selling begets more selling. Once we flipped into negative gamma, dynamic hedging kicked in. Mix that with the defensive rotation and you get violent, one-directional moves.

I’m watching three levels on SPY:

- Week-to-date VWAP for bounce resistance - around $679.80
- Lower weekly implied move - around $680 can be bounce resistance 
- 5-day moving average - still declining, needs to flatten before any sustained rally

We’re slightly negative on the year now. Everyone who bought SPY since January 1st on a volume-weighted basis is underwater. That becomes a psychological resistance level.

- Most Mag 7 Names Hit Their Weekly Targets

NVDA broke below its weekly implied move around $179.50. GOOGL tagged $320, bounced briefly, then gave it back. AAPL is outside its weekly range with that 2-3 standard deviation daily move. MSFT tagged the lower weekly and held just above. META closed next to its $603.50 lower bound. TSLA broke below $420 with upside resistance at $437.50.

Here’s what’s interesting - we’re actually not that far from what the market priced for weekly volatility. If these names close back inside their weekly ranges, this isn’t a major structural break. It’s elevated volatility within expected parameters.

But if we continue lower and stay outside these ranges? Different story.

- Trading Implications

If you’re short, you’re already outside weekly implied moves on SPY and most Mag 7 names. Initiating shorts after a 3SD move means you’re chasing. You want positioning before the move or after consolidation, not in the middle of capitulation.

If you’re looking long, wait for structure:

- Let the 5-day MA flatten and turn up
- Watch for consolidation near resistance
- Reclaim of year-to-date VWAP matters


- What I’m Thinking

Defensive rotation is accelerating. Staples, utilities, healthcare are leading while growth lags. The divergence between consumer staples and SPY is real. Overbought indicators are resolving down. We flipped into negative gamma. We’re outside weekly implied moves on major indices. AAPL had a 3-standard deviation move that typically requires a news catalyst.

This isn’t the time to force longs. The market spent weeks setting up these warning signs. The sector rotation, the divergences, the overbought readings - they all played out exactly as the playbook suggests.

We could bounce back into weekly ranges short-term. But the distribution pattern is textbook. When staples outperform at SPY highs, when defensive sectors lead, when you’re in negative gamma territory - you respect that.

- This Week’s Calendar

Economic data: GDP, personal income, and personal spending Thursday. PMI flash numbers Friday.

Earnings: Netflix reported today. We’ve got IBKR, JNJ, HAL, PG, and INTC this week. Nothing market-moving. The heavy tech earnings start next week.

The real question is whether we reclaim these weekly implied moves and hold them, or if we continue breaking down with volume.

The signs were there. They still are.

### Post #89: 2026-01-23 03:24

**Understanding Exercisable Warrants: The ONDS Deep Dive**

Ok so earlier today @Drone_Daddy  made a post and used the word exercisable warrant. A lot of people did not know what that meant so I decided to make a post. 

Let’s dive in ladies and gentlemen.
-----

What is an “Exercisable Warrant”?

A warrant is a contract issued by a company that gives the holder the right, but not the obligation, to buy the company’s stock at a specific price (**strike price**) before a certain date (**expiration date**).

When a warrant is “exercisable,” it means the holder has reached the window where they are legally allowed to convert that warrant into a share of stock.

**Key Terms to Know:**

**Strike Price:** The set price you pay to buy the share (e.g., $28.00 for ONDS).

**Expiration:** The date the warrant becomes worthless if not used (7 years for the new ONDS warrants).

**Dilution:** Because the company issues *new* shares when a warrant is exercised, the total number of shares increases, which can “dilute” the ownership percentage of existing shareholders.

-----

The ONDS Deep Dive: January 2026 Warrants

On **January 12, 2026**, Ondas Inc. closed a landmark $1 billion offering. This deal introduced a massive number of warrants into the market. If you are looking at ONDS right now, you are likely seeing two distinct types of warrants:

**1. Common Stock Warrants ($28.00 Strike)**

Ondas issued approximately **121.5 million** of these warrants.

**Status:** Immediately exercisable.

**Strike Price:** $28.00.

**Current Market Context:** As of today, ONDS common stock is trading around **$12.62**.

**What this means:** These warrants are currently **“Out of the Money” (OTM)**. Since the market price ($12.62) is lower than the strike price ($28.00), no rational investor would exercise them right now because it’s cheaper to buy shares on the open market.

**2. Pre-Funded Warrants ($0.0001 Strike)**

Ondas also issued about **41.7 million** pre-funded warrants.

**Why they exist:** These are typically used by institutional investors who want the economic equivalent of owning the stock but need to avoid certain regulatory “ownership caps” (like the 9.9% rule).

**Strike Price:** Essentially zero ($0.0001).

**What this means:** These act almost exactly like real shares. The investor has already “pre-paid” for them, and they can be converted into common stock at any time for a fraction of a penny.

-----

How Exercise Works: Cash vs. Cashless

If ONDS stock were to climb to $40.00, warrant holders would likely want to exercise. They generally have two choices:

**Cash Exercise:** Holder pays $28.00 in cash per warrant. Receives 1 full share. ONDS receives $28.00 in fresh capital.

**Cashless Exercise:** Holder “trades in” the value of the warrants to cover the cost. Receives a smaller number of “net” shares. ONDS receives $0 cash, but issues fewer shares.

If all 121 million common warrants were exercised for cash, Ondas could potentially raise an additional **$3.4 billion** in gross proceeds, which they’ve stated would be used for “Core+ Strategic Growth” and acquisitions.

-----

What This Means for Today

As of **January 22, 2026**, the ONDS warrants represent a “wait and see” scenario:

**For Shareholders:** There is significant “overhang.” If the stock price nears $28, many warrants could be converted, increasing the share count and potentially slowing the stock’s upward momentum.

**For Warrant Holders:** Your warrants are a 7-year bet. You are hoping that by 2033, the technology behind Ondas Autonomous Systems (OAS) and their private wireless networks drives the stock price well above $28.00.

**For the Company:** They have secured a $1 billion “war chest” and have the potential for billions more in the future if they execute their business plan and the stock price rises.

### Post #91: 2026-01-24 18:00 — `$SMH, $SPY, $VIX`

**You asked I answered: How I Plan for the Week Ahead 💰**

Last week a lot of you asked about weekend planning and how I prepare for the upcoming week. I promised a reply so let’s get to it.

## Rule #1: Take a Real Day Off

Use either Saturday or Sunday as a true rest day. Enjoy life, family, friends, your kids. We’re not robots and at the end of the day we came into this world with nothing and you will leave with nothing. Learn to enjoy life and not stress about money.

## Rule #2: Mental Reflection (15-20 Minutes)

Now that you had your day off we use the other day to prepare. Start with a simple mental reflection of the past week. What went well, what didn’t. What mistakes did you make? Where can you improve.

Don’t spend too long on it you don’t need crazy fancy journal skills. The point is to remind yourself of the good and bad. On a psychological level for us to improve we must iterate through our mistakes. A lot of the best athletes have mentioned when they would start out they would actually re-watch recordings of their games to see where they fucked up.

## Rule #3: Market Structure Analysis

This is where we actually jump into the charts. I start with looking at 5 things in terms of what the market did last week and my expectation for this upcoming week:

**$VIX, $SPY, $SMH, XLY/XLP & JNK/TLT**

I’m sure you all know the first 3 but few may even know you can do ratios in TradingView or what they mean. Here’s a brief summary:

**XLY/XLP - Risk On/Off Gauge**
Consumer Discretionary vs Staples

- Bullish: Ratio rising (discretionary beating staples). Confidence, spending.
- Bearish: Ratio falling (staples beating discretionary). Risk off, defensive.

**JNK/TLT - Credit Stress Indicator**
High yield bonds vs Treasury bonds

- Bullish: Rising = Confidence, yield seeking
- Bearish: Falling = Flight to safety, fear rising
- Why it’s essential: Bond market often moves BEFORE stocks

## This Week’s Full Overview

**$VIX - NEUTRAL**
VIX rallied to 21 and then rejected all the way back down to 16. We got the usual Trump tweet fuckery as fear gained then fell. For us to continue to ATHs we actually want to see VIX continue to slowly drop lower towards 13. If we see Monday we close above 16 it means more volatility and potentially back down we go.

**$SPY - NEUTRAL**
Beginning of the week SPY broke into Negative Gamma and nuked below $680. Trump scratched his left nut made a tweet and Wednesday we were back up. People started getting super bullish again and then we just consolidated for 2 days.

From options flows I will say there was a lot of bullish flows including those from whales. No that does not automatically make us bullish and believe it or not whales can be wrong as well.

For the expiration of the next OPEX 02/20 we are just above $689 which is our HVL. What is the HVL you may ask? The High Volatility Level (HVL) in options trading is a critical, dynamic threshold often used in MenthorQ’s gamma models that signifies a shift in market gamma, acting as a boundary between stable, low volatility conditions (positive gamma) and volatile, high volatility conditions (negative gamma).

Let’s keep it very simple we are an inch above in positive gamma so I will call it neutral. Above $689 we rally and probably get another ATH and below we either chop or nuke.

**$SMH - BULLISH**
Why SMH? Well this is where we can apply Modern Dow Theory and semiconductors are vital to the modern “digital” economy (the new “industrials”). If you look at the past 5 years any time SPY has ripped faces higher it was led by SMH. The only exceptions were times before a correction.

The chart is very simple on this one. Above $290 we stay bullish. Below we chop or may see a larger correction.

**XLY/XLP - BEARISH**
We had a nice bounce but overall it is showing a bearish trend on the daily and the start of consolidation on the weekly. Basically overall the market is confused about what the fuck is actually going to happen next. We are not completely freaking out but Trump seems to keep us all on our toes.

**JNK/TLT - BULLISH**
Why do I like this one? Well because it’s an Early Warning System. If junk bonds (JNK) begin to sell off while Treasuries (TLT) rise, it suggests that professional credit investors are anticipating defaults or an economic slowdown before equity investors. To me this chart still looks good although we did get some selling this week but not enough to break us out of market structure.

## The Verdict

Let’s put all the pieces of the puzzle together: Neutral, Neutral, Bullish, Bearish, Bullish that leaves us with… **NEUTRAL**

Ladies and Gentlemen we are basically at an indecision point and candle closes will be important. Our goal is to see a dropping VIX and SPY above that $689 level. We are cautious and looking for good setups on strong companies.

Now based on having a neutral to realistically bearish bias (since Trump keeps starting shit worldwide) I next go on to picking my CSPs for this week on stocks I want to own. That will be its own post due to the amount of content I will need to add. Keep an eye out for that when it comes it will be good.

## My Trading Philosophy

For those of you who don’t know I am an options seller. I have chosen this strategy because it brings me peace and I am ok with being patient. With my current calculations by the time I am 30 I should be sitting at a networth of $500K plus minus following my strategy + work + side business income.

Nothing to brag about and some will say that makes me a broke bitch, but I will answer that money does not make a man it amplifies who he already is. If you are a happy person who is grateful and has good connections with the people around you, you don’t need millions to live an awesome life.

Wishing you all an amazing weekend!
