### Post #92: 2026-02-04 03:35

**Big Tech Selloff While Defensives Rally - What’s Happening? 🤷‍♂️📉**

First things first apologies for the lack of posts. It’s a combination of this app being filled with crap and drama lately, irl things, and a project I am working on releasing soon before end of February. Now back to the post. 

We’re seeing a rare market rotation pattern that’s only occurred twice before - right before the dot-com crash and last year’s tariff tantrum. Big tech and semiconductors are bleeding while defensive sectors (staples, utilities, healthcare) rally hard. This is a late-cycle signal worth watching closely.

The Warning Signal
Historical data shows something critical: when tech sectors drop while staple sectors rally, the stats get ugly fast. Looking at performance 6-9-12 months out after this pattern, the data is consistently negative.

What’s changing:
	∙	Tech leadership weakening since Fed cuts began in September
	∙	Money rotating into energy, materials, staples, industrials
	∙	Magnificent 7 losing momentum
	∙	Market breadth expanding (usually a late-cycle sign)

Semiconductors Leading the Decline
Key observation: When semiconductors fail, the whole market typically follows. They’re the canary in the coal mine.

Recent semiconductor action:
	∙	AMD down ~6% after earnings despite revenue beat
	∙	Overall semiconductor weakness across the board
	∙	SMH showing concerning technical breakdown
This matters because semiconductors historically lead major market moves.

The Magnificent 7 Problem
Post-earnings damage:
	∙	Microsoft getting destroyed
	∙	NVDA starting to wobble
	∙	Tesla shaky at $420
	∙	Meta gave back gap-fill gains
	∙	PayPal crashed 20% on earnings miss

Critical context: The S&P 500 concentration is extreme - top 10 stocks make up 37% of the index. If Magnificent 7 stocks continue underperforming, the index can’t rally even if breadth improves.

Hindenburg Omen Clusters
We’re seeing fresh Hindenburg Omen signals - technical indicators that have historically preceded major market tops including the 2021 highs and previous crashes. While not a standalone sell signal, when these cluster together with other warnings (defensive rotation, falling truck sales, weakening semiconductors), they demand attention.

The Consumer Weakness Story
Leading indicators breaking down:
	∙	XLY vs XLP (consumer discretionary vs staples) broke below support
	∙	This ratio tracks roughly 1/3 of the US economy
	∙	Signal: American consumer starting to weaken
	∙	Implication: Late-cycle dynamics intensifying

Earnings Season Reality Check
The pattern emerging:
	∙	Beats with strong guidance = stocks rally
	∙	Misses or weak guidance = brutal selloffs (see PayPal -20%)
	∙	Market has zero tolerance for disappointment
Still to report: Amazon, Alphabet, ARM, Qualcomm - these will be critical tests for whether tech can regain leadership.

Sector Rotation in Action
What’s working:
	∙	Energy stocks hitting new highs (major breakout)
	∙	Defensive staples (XLP) skyrocketing
	∙	Materials continuing strength
	∙	Gold recovering after historic silver crash
What’s not working:
	∙	Software (IGV) - 6 consecutive down days
	∙	Big tech broadly under pressure
	∙	Semiconductors showing weakness
	∙	Growth stocks losing premium

The 7,000 Resistance Problem
S&P 500 has now hit and rejected 7,000 twice. Multiple Fibonacci extensions from various timeframes (2022 lows, 2024 tariff tantrum, even GFC lows) all converge at this level.
Technical setup:
	∙	Major psychological resistance
	∙	Heavy call option walls at 7K
	∙	Put walls building at 6,875-6,900
	∙	Break below 6,900 could trigger waterfall selling

Bitcoin and Crypto Correlation
BTC tested $73K and bounced to barely hold $75K. Key context: MicroStrategy’s average entry is around $76K - this is a critical support zone.

Watch levels:
	∙	$79,200 = resistance confirming buyer commitment
	∙	$75K = must hold or next stop is $67-68K
	∙	44% of Bitcoin supply now underwater

What This Means for Trading
Current market state:
	∙	Stock picker’s market, not index market
	∙	Sector rotation is the primary strategy
	∙	Tech requires selective, tactical plays
	∙	Defensive positioning rewarded
	∙	Earnings volatility extreme

Key codes to watch:
	∙	SMH vs SPY (semiconductors vs market)
	∙	XLY vs XLP (consumer health)
	∙	XLK vs XLP (tech vs defensives - just broke down)

The Fed Chair Wildcard
New Fed chair announcement adds uncertainty. Potential shift from Powell’s “prevent problems before they happen” approach to a more reactive “fix problems after they break” strategy.

Implication: Could mean less consistent liquidity support, more volatility, and larger drawdowns before intervention.

What’s NOT Broken Yet
Watching for additional warnings:
	∙	Bonds haven’t freaked out yet
	∙	Dollar still testing multi-year trendline
	∙	Financials haven’t rolled over completely
	∙	10-year yields rising but not spiking

When these dominoes start falling alongside current weakness, that’s when late-cycle becomes end-of-cycle.

Bottom Line
This isn’t time to panic, but it IS time to pay attention. The market is sending clear late-cycle signals:
- Leadership rotating from growth to defensives
- Semiconductors weakening
- Consumer indicators breaking down
- Hindenburg Omen clusters forming
- Tech earnings misses getting destroyed
- Index resistance at major technical confluence

For now: Respect the rotation. Follow the money into energy, materials, and defensive sectors. Be selective in tech. Watch semiconductors closely - they’ll tell us if this is just rotation or the start of something bigger.

The easy money in tech has been made. Now it gets harder.

### Post #93: 2026-02-07 15:06 — `$IREN, $GLD, $ONDS, $HOOD, $GLXY, $RR, $SLV`

**This Week Was Market Tuition – And It’s Ok If You Paid**

If you felt pain this week, you’re not alone. If you questioned everything, that’s normal. If you wanted to puke looking at your portfolio, welcome to the fucking club.

—The Reality Check—
While the S&P casually dipped 2%, the stuff may of us actually trade got obliterated. Popular plays like $ONDS, $GLXY, $IREN, $RR – nuked. $SLV and $GLD? Went Down 25%. Bitcoin touched $61K and the fear was real. Margin calls. Capitulation. Don’t even get me started on $HOOD ….. bought 100 shares at $85 thinking wow this is great and then it went to $70 lol. 

I watched my wheel strategy portfolio go from up double digits on the year to down 14% at the worst point. Fourteen percent. On positions I run for weekly income. That’s not supposed to happen, but it did.

Then Friday shows up with a face-ripping 10% green candle on most names, and suddenly half those losses evaporate. Breakeven feels like a win.

—Here’s What This Week Taught Me (Again)—
This isn’t about being a technical analysis god or predicting every move. It’s about three things:

1. Conviction Without Delusion
I believed in my positions enough not to panic sell the bottom. But I’m not an idiot – I used Friday’s bounce to trim and raise cash. Green days are gifts to reduce risk, not invitations to go bigger.

2. Position Sizing Saves You
The reason I survived a 14% drawdown is because I didn’t have stupid position sizes going in. If you got margin called or blown out, the market just taught you about risk management. It’s an expensive lesson, but it’s one you won’t forget.

3. Emotional Control Is Everything
Fear makes you sell bottoms. Greed makes you buy tops. The traders who survive aren’t the ones who feel nothing – they’re the ones who feel everything but act based on their plan, not their panic.

—For Everyone Who Took Losses—
If you sold at the current bottom (I’m skeptical of Friday’s price action but will follow price action), don’t beat yourself up. The market is designed to make you feel maximum pain right before it reverses. That’s not your fault – that’s how markets work.

But here’s the thing: every loss is tuition if you extract the lesson. Ask yourself:
	∙	Was my position too big?
	∙	Did I have a stop or was I hoping?
	∙	Did I panic or did I execute my plan?
	∙	What will I do differently next time?
The market doesn’t punish you for losing trades. It punishes you for not learning from them.

—Where I’m At Now—
As of right now, I’m down 4.3% YTD. Not great, not terrible. I used Friday to close positions at small profits or breakeven and move to more cash.

Am I bullish? No. Am I bearish? Not really. I expect more chop, maybe another leg down. But I’m ready for whatever comes because I have dry powder and a clear head.

—The Real Game—
The market doesn’t care about your feelings. It doesn’t care if you’re down or up. It just keeps moving.
Your job isn’t to predict it perfectly. Your job is to survive the chaos long enough to be there when opportunity shows up.
This week separated the players from the spectators. If you’re still here, still analyzing, still learning – you’re already ahead. Most people blow up and quit. You didn’t.

—We’re All in This Together—
This community exists because we share the pain and the wins. We don’t bullshit each other with fake screenshots or only show the highlights. We put our money where our mouth is and we show up even when it hurts.
Next week is a new week. New setups. New chances. But same lesson: size your positions right, stay emotionally stable, and play to survive first, win second.

If you need to take a break, take it. If you need to go to cash, do it. There’s no shame in stepping back to reset. The market will be here when you’re ready.

Wishing you all an amazing weekend 🤝

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bhpV/this-week-was-market-tuition-a*

### Post #94: 2026-02-08 17:09

**Every Single Insider Sold Last Week**

Clickbait title…. Or is it really? Well unfortunately my friends over 80% of the top insider transactions last week on dozens of S&P companies from directors and officers were indeed sells. The market’s broadening out, different sectors are moving, and we should expect way more volatility heading into midterms.

— Central bank liquidity is still expanding —
That’s helping keep things afloat. But the new Fed chair is talking about pulling back transparency on future policy moves. I actually don’t hate that idea of getting less hand-holding from the Fed but it does mean more uncertainty and more vol.

— Credit spreads haven’t spiked —
This matters. Bonds aren’t freaking out. Credit markets are stable. When credit spreads stay calm, it usually means we shouldn’t be panicking either. The debt market often sees problems before equities do, and right now it’s not flashing red.

— But here’s the thing the market’s completely split —

—What’s working:
Energy is absolutely ripping (been calling this for months and it keeps delivering)
Hardware and semiconductors still strong
Basically anything that isn’t software

—What’s dying:
Software/tech down 17% this year
Communication services down 4.7%
The Mag 7 is actually NEGATIVE while the S&P is somehow still up

Think about that. The seven biggest stocks are underwater but the index is positive. This is not a “buy everything” market anymore. You need to know exactly what you’re in and why.

— The Mag 7 to SPY ratio keeps weakening —
If you’re long big tech expecting it to carry the market like 2023-2024, you need to rethink that. Component correlation is breaking down similar to the early 2000s. Lots of stocks making new highs while others sink to new lows. That’s late-cycle behavior, not early-cycle.

The Mag 7 barely grabbed support. S&P held that 6,768 put level we’ve been watching. NASDAQ clutched 24,000. Bitcoin bounced right off that 62-68K demand zone.

IBIT put volume exploded to 1.37 million. $284 million in share volume. That’s massive activity, and it came right as Bitcoin broke into that 60-68K demand zone.

The stats back up the bounce too. When Bitcoin capitulates like this so three straight days down 3% each the 5-day recovery averages +10% about 80% of the time. We basically did that in one day.

— Let’s talk Bullish —
Since 2025 closed positive, history says the next 12 months are positive 46 out of 49 times. That’s a strong statistical edge for bulls in 2026. But remember—it’s a midterm year, which brings its own volatility patterns.

— The jobs market is normalizing —
Jobs openings per unemployed worker dropped from 2.0 down to about 0.7. That’s not a crash, just coming back to earth after the insanity of 2022. But the weakness is concentrated software engineers are getting hit first as AI starts having real impact on hiring.
It’s worth noting though: 0.7 and below has historically been a concern level. We’re right there now.

— Let’s talk Bearish —
JPM showing potential distribution with all those insider sales up at these levels. If it breaks $290, that’s a problem. Where financials go, the market usually follows.

Nvidia sitting at $170. If that breaks, there’s a nasty head and shoulders pattern on the biggest stock in the world. That would get ugly fast.

The 10-year yield is back up and stabilizing, which could actually be good for oil. Energy keeps working.

— Earnings season has been decent —
Not amazing, not terrible just decent. It’s keeping the market afloat even with everything else going on. Big names this week include Datadog and Shopify, both with expected moves of 15%+ on earnings.

The fact that earnings is almost an afterthought right now tells you something. We’ve had huge sector rotation into materials, metals, energy—stuff that’s completely overshadowed earnings beats and misses.

— A note on silver —
After that blowoff move we just saw, I’m more cautious here. When silver spikes like that, the historical stats are mediocre at best for what comes next. I’m sticking with gold as the preferred metals play. Silver might do some big percentage moves, but the risk/reward shifted after that freight train rally.

— Chinese stocks holding up —
They bounced right off support at 267 and remain in an upward trend heading into Chinese New Year, which is typically a positive seasonal period for them. Something to keep on the radar.

— Levels I’m tracking —
SPX 6,920-6,900 is stacked with puts is a huge support zone
IBIT at $40 (Bitcoin equivalent of $70K)
Gold back near $450 holding up
Silver in that $68-70 zone

— Bottom line —
We’re not in 2023-2024 anymore. The easy “buy tech and chill” trade is over. The market hit average bull run metrics. Now it’s all about asset selection.

Energy continues to crush. Big tech is struggling. Market breadth is all over the place. Semiconductors are still holding which is key for this market to stay alive.

The market held critical levels last week. Credit spreads are calm. Earnings are decent enough. But don’t confuse a bounce with a new bull leg starting.

We’re in a stock picker’s market now. Different sectors are doing completely different things. Know what you own, know why you own it, and don’t expect everything to move together like the last few years.

Midterms bring volatility. Position accordingly 🤝

Utilities rallied hard while everything else dumped. This is defensive rotation in action.

This is not 2024 when the Fed cut rates and big tech went ballistic. Now we’re seeing tech drop while midcaps, emerging markets, energy, healthcare, and utilities all move higher.

The market is finding new safe havens. And when that happens, you’re usually in late-cycle territory.

— Amazon is in a bear market —
Down over 20% from the top. Apple shed 5% in 24 hours after hitting resistance. Most tech is weak across the board.

We’re still in a rangebound market. The S&P is only 1.5% down from highs. But the flow underneath is concerning.

— Key levels I’m watching —
SPX 6,800 is the critical put wall. Tons of puts stacked there. If we break below, we could see narratives as low as 6,500. That would trigger negative gamma a waterfall effect where the market has to hedge itself aggressively.

NASDAQ 600 on the Q’s. Doesn’t matter which expiration you look at this is a massive put zone. We rallied off it once, now we’re back testing it. That’s concerning. The second test is always the one you question.

IBIT at $37 (Bitcoin around $62K support). $35 is also important—that’s the 62K level for Bitcoin.

Silver at the 618 fib level after dropping 10% in 24 hours. Massive puts came in around $70, which is now above current price.

— The American consumer is weakening —
This is one-third of the US economy, and it’s showing real strain.

This is starting to show up in credit card figures too.

— What worries me most —
The American consumer is dropping off, but bonds aren’t freaking out. Usually you’d see both if something catastrophic was coming. This suggests the consumer is strained and jobs reports confirm it.

Financials versus SPY are really weakening. That’s late-cycle behavior, not early-cycle.

— We got our fourth (maybe fifth) Hindenburg Omen —
One Hindenburg Omen doesn’t mean much. But a cluster of them? That’s when you start paying attention.

We’re now at four or five depending on how you measure it. This typically signals volatility ahead which is not necessarily a crash, but definitely choppier markets for the next couple months.

Remember, this is a midterm election year. They tend to be volatile with two selloffs. First quarter weakness between late January and March is pretty normal. The question is whether this is the end or just a dip worth buying.

— Crypto hit extreme fear —
The fear and greed index is sitting at 5 right now. That’s extreme fear territory.

Looking at historical crashes—the MT Gox crash had us at 11, the 2017-18 crash went even lower. We’re at 5 right now with Bitcoin just above $60K.

Markets are packing in extreme greed and extreme fear incredibly quickly. Flow levels matter more than sentiment at this point.

— The stat that matters —
Over the last eight sessions, 115 stocks in the S&P declined around 7%. When we’ve seen this pattern at previous peaks, the market went down 34% overall.

This is a huge deal when you break down the similarities between 2026 and the dot-com era. We’ve got the AI boom, concerns about massive hardware buildouts, and tech companies floating 100-year bonds while burning through cash on data centers. Google, Meta, all of them—spending billions with no clear ROI yet.

And now IPOs are getting pulled forward. SpaceX, OpenAI, Anthropic—all trying to go public earlier than expected. What do these guys know that we don’t?

— A note on silver —
After that massive spike, the Nordlyst research showed that historically, silver tends to chop sideways after crashes like this—not deliver exceptional gains.

— Bottom line —
The market structure is starting to look sick. The AI economy is now the US economy in many ways, and if we lose key levels on the Q’s at 600 or the Mag 7 breaks down further, this becomes a real story.

It’s still an abundance mindset if you’re in the right sectors. But this isn’t buy-everything-and-hold anymore.

We’re 1.5% off highs on the SPX, but underneath the hood, 115 stocks are down 7% in eight sessions. That’s the same pattern we saw before a 34% decline in 2000.

Stay sharp. Know what you own. 2026 is shaping up to be one for the history books.

Not financial advice. Just sharing the data and what I’m seeing in the flows. This market rewards selectivity and punishes complacency.

### Post #96: 2026-02-14 16:59

**Happy Valentines Day: The role of men in our society**

Opened the app yesterday saw drama over scammers, fake call outs, indicators, and more irrelevant shit. I don’t partake in any of it. Not my goal. When Bobdog launches his indicator suite I will challenge him for healthy competition not because I originally planned to. Now back to the post. 

Today is V Day so let’s talk about what it means to be a man. Not the performative bullshit on social media.

Here’s the simple truth we all know as men: a man doesn’t need love the way a woman does. He needs respect.

—
Love is easy to give. It’s emotional, it’s fluid, it flows when things are good. But respect? Respect is earned through action. Through sacrifice. Through showing up day after day when everything in you wants to quit.

A man can feel loved and still be completely broken inside if he doesn’t feel respected.

—
The role of a man in a family isn’t complicated. Provide, Protect, and Lead.

Provide means more than just money, though that matters. It means creating stability. Safety. A foundation where your family doesn’t have to worry about the basics so they can focus on becoming who they’re meant to be.

Protect means being the shield. From physical threats, yes. But also from chaos, from uncertainty, from the thousand small dangers that eat away at peace of mind.

Lead means making the hard decisions. Taking responsibility when things go wrong. Being the rock that doesn’t move when storms come.

—
But here’s what needs to be said too: being a rock doesn’t mean you’re not human.

You can be strong and still struggle. You can be the provider and still have days where you don’t know how you’re going to make it work. You can be the protector and still feel vulnerable.

That’s not weakness. That’s reality.

—
The weight of responsibility is heavy. Some days it crushes you. You wake up and the pressure of being everything to everyone feels impossible.

And in those moments, it’s okay to feel it. To acknowledge it. To tell your brother “I’m going through it right now.”
We’re human. We break. We doubt. We feel the full weight of what we’ve taken on.

—
What we got wrong wasn’t telling boys to have emotions. It was telling them that having emotions means you stop being a man.

That’s bullshit.

You can feel everything and still show up. You can have moments where you want to quit and still keep going. You can ask for support from your
 brothers and still be the leader your family needs.
—
Western culture has spent the last 50 years systematically destroying this framework.

We’ve been told that masculinity is “toxic.” That wanting to be the provider makes you a controlling patriarch. That protection is patronizing. That leadership is oppression.
We’ve feminized boys from kindergarten onwards. Punished them for their natural aggression and energy. Told them to sit still, be quiet, express their feelings, and apologize for taking up space.

Then we act surprised when men in their 20s and 30s are lost. Directionless. Living in their parents’ basement playing video games and watching porn instead of building empires.

—
The woman in your life doesn’t need another friend.
What she needs and what she craves on a biological level even if she’d never admit it is a man she can respect. 

A man who handles his business. A man who doesn’t need her approval to know his own worth. A man who can say no and tell her when she is wrong even when she does not want to hear it.

Respect first. Love follows.

—
But respect doesn’t mean you pretend to be invincible.
Real strength is carrying weight while being honest about the cost. It’s being the leader even when you’re exhausted. It’s protecting your family even when you’re scared.
The strongest men I know aren’t the ones who never struggle. They’re the ones who struggle and keep going anyway.

—
The single most destructive lie of modern culture is that men and women are the same. That we want the same things, need the same things, operate the same way.

We don’t.

A woman needs to feel safe, cherished, and valued. A man needs to feel respected, trusted, and needed.
Different fuel, different engines.

—
Money amplifies character but doesn’t build it. The same applies to relationships.

You can’t buy respect. You can’t negotiate it. You can’t manipulate or charm your way into it.

You earn it through consistent action over time. By doing what you said you’d do. By being a man of your word.

—
The role of a man in the family isn’t about power. It’s about responsibility.

Power is taking. Responsibility is carrying weight so others don’t have to.

Every successful family I know has a man at the center who takes responsibility for outcomes. Not a dictator. Not a tyrant. But someone who says “this is on me” when things go wrong and “we did this together” when things go right.

—
And when one of my brothers is going through hardship, I support them. No judgment. No telling them to “man up” or bury it.

We all go through tough times. The job loss. The relationship ending. The business failing. The days where you can’t see a way forward.

In those moments, you need your brothers. The ones who know the weight you carry. The ones who can sit with you in the darkness and remind you that this isn’t the end.
That’s not weakness. That’s brotherhood.

—
Western culture wants to convince you that this is outdated. That we’ve evolved past these “traditional roles.”
But biology doesn’t give a fuck about your politics.

Women are still attracted to strength, competence, and leadership. Men are still driven by purpose, achievement, and respect.

We can pretend otherwise. We can build entire ideologies around denying these truths.
But reality always wins eventually.

—
If you’re a man reading this and you feel lost, here’s what I’d tell you:

Find something worth building. A business, a skill, a body, a family. Something that demands you become better than you are today.

Then build it. Day by day. Rep by rep. Dollar by dollar.
Don’t wait for permission. Don’t wait for perfect conditions. Don’t wait until you “feel ready.”

You build respect through action, not contemplation.

—
And when it gets hard, because it will get hard, remember this:
You’re allowed to struggle. You’re allowed to feel it. You’re allowed to need support.

You’re human.
But you’re also a man. Which means when morning comes, you get up and you handle your business.

That’s the balance. Feel everything. Process it. Lean on your brothers when you need to.

Then get back to work.
—

Respect yourself first. The rest follows. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Trading_with_Art/bhRH/happy-valentines-day-the-role-*

### Post #97: 2026-02-19 04:18

**Silver’s February 27th Delivery Squeeze And Is Big Money Back Into Tech?**

We are seeing big money flipping back into tech after two sessions of extreme selling. But here’s the bigger story nobody’s talking about: the world’s second most important metal for market liquidity is showing serious strain heading into next week.

— Tech got a relief bid after extreme shorting —

Short interest on tech stocks hit an extreme level. Then the markets fell even more, which makes a case that maybe tech has been overshorted.

Two sessions now of movements out of defensives and back into tech. We were selling tech so hard that somebody went, “Wait a second, there could be a relief rally here.”

Microsoft getting a slight bid. We’ve got a gap fill and the market is structuring. We did see a slight higher high. Haven’t broken the 618 fib yet, but somebody tried to put a bid in.

Russell finding some strength too. Slight bids over the last 24 hours.

— The S&P found support at the put wall —

6,770-6,780 was the critical put wall. The market bounced right off it. That’s a good sign. The market is supporting its own base and saying, “We don’t want a waterfall effect.”
6,800 remains the main put level.

— NASDAQ’s 600 level held —

595-600 on the Q’s. Everyone stacked puts there. Hard to get under. If it does, it creates a waterfall event. But generally, you find bids around these points from a technical perspective, and we got a small bid here.

— Corporate AI disruption fears are rising —

Mentions of “artificial intelligence disruption” are spiking in corporate management discussions. A lot of people believe AI is going to take out jobs in a big way.

The biggest companies in the world are projected to spend even more insanely by 2028. You can see the overall cash burn into the economy. These tech stocks had a lot of money. Now they’re burning it. That’s causing concern.

— Tech earnings have actually been exceptional —

They’ve been giving pretty good earnings. In fact, exceptional ones. So the question is: Does AI give us the results? If it does, the market will be okay. If it doesn’t, the market will have a hiccup, to say the least.

— Oil is breaking out —

Oil has been very strong on charts. A lot of people missed this one. Oil stocks in particular have been crushing.
UK oil is about to break a new high. There’s not much on the left-hand side stopping oil from potentially going ballistic. This is all in the wake of geopolitical tensions flaring up.
JP Morgan held the low. Got a little break above $64 a barrel. UK oil is the stronger chart.

— Semiconductors remain the bellwether —

Nvidia’s still holding lows, hasn’t broken highs. Nothing major to note. But semiconductors need to be monitored because if they weaken too much, that’s a problem. Right now, they’re still pretty strong.

— Bitcoin at a very interesting structure zone —

Back at $66K. Very strong demand on the left side. The market has wicked off the bottom a few times.
The market is consolidating after a freight train down. To stop a freight train often takes structure. Bitcoin’s at a good level, good range. A lot of people turning super negative now like really super negative. But from the chart perspective, it’s a very interesting structure zone.

Key level to watch: $72K. That’s probably the most important zone. On the downside, a wipe of the previous low would need a very quick bid.

IBIT is still struggling to break through the $40 level, which is where we go back into positive gamma. Lots of puts sitting here. Rangebound until proven otherwise.

— Now here’s the silver story —

ComEx silver inventory is draining fast heading into the February 27th expiration. Total inventory in the vaults is showing a significant decline. This has people speculating we could see a short squeeze on the second biggest precious metal.

Here’s why it matters: Most people roll paper contracts. There’s usually not enough physical for delivery if everyone demands it. The cutoff is February 27th, but we’re already seeing a drain that’s more significant than anything in previous years.

— CME withdrawals are accelerating —

We’ve lost 458,152 ounces in recent withdrawals. Another 673,739 ounces. Then 1.947 million ounces from JP Morgan alone.

This isn’t necessarily unusual, but it’s one of the biggest drawdowns we’ve seen in terms of pressure on the private metals market in quite some time.

— Investor demand for precious metals is skyrocketing —

UBS data shows investor demand for precious metals absolutely exploded in the latter half of last year. There were lines for physical. Everybody went ballistic.

Strong central bank buying continues. Top buyers: Russia, China, Turkey, Poland, India. Turkey’s in there because of currency concerns. When you have currency concerns or fiat problems, people go to the world’s oldest currency aka gold or silver.

China has also blocked silver from leaving the country anymore.

— Silver’s key level: $80 —

That would be a higher high, which could signal a bid coming in. It’s been very volatile after a huge move, so expect volatility. To stop a freight train like this, you need to create a lot of base.

Current puts are stacked at $69 for this week, with $73 on the call side. Those two levels are worth watching.
Silver is at a great demand level and that’s why you’re seeing action here. But be careful. The easiest money in silver has been made. Now it becomes harder as we see the ramifications of that explosion last month.

— My take on the squeeze potential —

Is this something to watch? Yeah. Is it something that instantly causes a squeeze? I don’t know about that. We arguably already saw a squeeze when silver went over $110 an ounce a month ago.

— Defensives had been dominating for months —

Since the Federal Reserve rate cut, we’ve seen movement out of tech and into defensives. The American consumer aka one-third of the US economy has been weakening.
Big money has been moving out of tech for four to five months. But now, slight bids over the last 24 hours back into tech. Doesn’t change the bigger picture, but structure is starting to appear.

— Bottom line —

Tech just got a relief bid after extreme shorting. Structure is starting to appear. Doesn’t change the bigger defensive rotation we’ve seen for months, but somebody decided to put a bid in.

Silver’s February 27th delivery could get interesting. The drain is real, but the big move may have already happened. Watch $80 for confirmation of a bid.

Oil is breaking out. Semiconductors holding strong. Bitcoin at critical structure.

It all comes down to AI. Does it deliver profits? If yes, market’s okay. If no, we’re in for a hiccup.

Auto-plots everything you need to make visual trading decisions. Two modes:

Fibonacci Mode detects your swing high and low automatically and plots all key fib levels. Extensions flip direction based on trend so the levels are always relevant to the current structure.

Zones & Pivots Mode detects support and resistance from pivot clusters and color-codes them live. Green = support, red = resistance, yellow = you’re inside a zone, be careful. When no S/R zone is nearby it falls back to Woodie pivots so you always have the closest levels on screen.

Shows you who is actually in control  aka buyers or sellers on every bar.

The volume bars split buying vs selling pressure in real time. The MA line on top is the signal: green means buyers are running it, red means sellers. It only flips at bar close so no repaint, no fake signals mid-bar. Backtested from the 1 minute chart all the way to the weekly.

Most volume indicators just show you a bar. This one tells you if that volume is meaningful.

— Part 3: TraderDaddy Pro Oscillator — 

This is the one I’m most proud of and the main reason for the $6.99 price.

It’s not one indicator it’s 12 internal indicators aggregated into a single line through weighted computation. Each signal was manually backtested and tuned independently across every timeframe. The 5min settings are completely different from the 1H, which are completely different from the daily. That work took months and AI couldn’t do it for me.

The output is simple: green above the line means the weight of evidence says buyers are in control. Magenta below means sellers. Gray in the middle means it’s choppy stay out. It won’t flip on one candle. It requires close confirmation, MA alignment, and enough signals agreeing before it changes state. No repaint.

This is where the real edge is. The Oscillator and Volume are designed to work as a two-confirmation system and you should never use one without the other.

Oscillator green + Volume flips green = long entry. Oscillator magenta + Volume flips red = short entry.

If the Oscillator is bullish but volume is still red, you wait. If volume flips green but the oscillator is sitting in neutral gray, you wait. Both have to line up. When they do, that’s the highest probability setup the suite produces.

### Post #99: 2026-02-25 03:38

**The Fulcrum Event That Could Trigger a Cascading Flow Shift 🚀**


Everything converges Wednesday after the close. The biggest stock in the world reports, and what happens at $195 could reshape market structure for months.

This isn’t just about one company’s results. This is about whether the AI narrative holds, whether gamma turns positive, and whether the market breaks out of the range it’s been stuck in for weeks to months.

— The gamma story everyone’s watching —

Options market pricing in a 6-7% move. Historically Nvidia moves 3.5-5%.

When actual volatility exceeds expectations, it creates pressure on options dealers who sold premium. That pressure compounds. For Nvidia specifically, the options market is screaming about $195 and $200. Those are the gamma-flip levels.

If Nvidia clears $195 after earnings, you’re likely looking at positive gamma acceleration up toward $210 and potentially $220. Each $10 interval is significant. When gamma flips positive, options dealers have to buy stock to hedge, which creates mechanical buying pressure independent of fundamentals.

Dark pool activity spiked in the last 24 hours across major ETFs. 

— Russell 2000 outperforming is the real signal —

Small caps have beaten the S&P 500 over the last three to six months. Something that hasn’t happened consistently since 2022.

When the Russell performs better than the Magnificent 7, it means leadership has broadened. The market isn’t being driven purely by mega-cap tech momentum anymore.

That’s not necessarily bullish. Broader participation can indicate a healthy market, but it can also indicate that large caps are losing their dominant advantage. Combined with financial weakness, it suggests the old order is breaking.

The data-center-and-AI-driven growth story is being challenged by macro concerns about consumer health and credit stress.

— Energy crushes, financials crumble —

Wall Street hasn’t been buying everything equally. Over the last three months, financials down 7% while oil services jumped 40%. Energy and gold solidly positive.

This isn’t random. This is flow telling a story: late-cycle rotation. Money is moving away from traditional financial stability plays and chasing inflation hedges and commodity strength.

But here’s the tension. Financials are technically breaking down. JP Morgan, the bellwether for the entire sector, barely held its support level.

If financials continue deteriorating while energy keeps climbing, it’s confirmation we’re in late-cycle consolidation. That’s not inherently a crash signal, but it means the market has stopped being monolithic. The Magnificent 7 dominance is fracturing.

— Tech short interest and the software squeeze —

Tech short interest accelerated throughout 2026. Software (IGV) has been heavily shorted and recently saw massive fund inflows from institutional players.

Standard play: everyone’s doing the same thing, so the market wipes them out. That’s exactly what happened. IGV made a lower low right after the fund flows reversed.

This creates opportunity tension. Software has been hated, but the selling momentum may be exhausted. The question isn’t whether software is good or bad. The question is whether the short-covering rally is real.

That answer depends on what happens with Nvidia. If Nvidia earnings confirm AI demand is real and accelerating, the narrative flips and software gets bought. If Nvidia disappoints or guides lower, short interest keeps compressing prices.

— Macro warnings clustering —

Housing data is deteriorating. Building permits hit their lowest level since 2019. Pending home sales declining. This matters because residential debt is the backbone of the U.S. economy. When housing begins stalling, financial stress follows.

Credit card delinquencies rising. Student loan delinquency rates climbing.

The good news: credit markets haven’t panicked yet. High-yield bonds haven’t crashed. Corporates aren’t screaming distress. Bonds ticked up slightly, but nothing catastrophic.
The market isn’t pricing in immediate systemic stress. Yet. That means the deterioration is slow-building, not acute.

— Silver, oil, and the inflation bid —

Silver broke above key resistance and is pressing toward $90. Gold recovering post-crash. Both moving on geopolitical tensions and general inflation hedge demand.

Oil has been a star performer over the last four months, jumping alongside energy services and exploration plays. The structure is clean: higher highs, higher lows, holding the daily 20 moving average. No breakdown signals yet.

This matters because commodities strength while financials weaken is textbook late-cycle behavior. Real assets are outperforming financial assets. That’s rotation, not capitulation.

— Key levels right now —

SPX trapped in a 200-point range between 6,800 and 7,000. The equilibrium sits at 6,900, the most traded level. Break above 6,900 and 7,000 becomes the next logical target. Drop below 6,800 and you open a channel trade down to 6,600.

Put walls sitting just underneath the 6,800 level. If that breaks, you get waterfall conditions. Remember: it’s an elevator down and stairs up.

For NASDAQ 100 (QQQ), 600 is the major threshold. Current level is above it, which is holding. The higher resistance sits at 617, which aligns with the Magnificent 7’s technical setup. Those mega-cap stocks have stabilized but haven’t confirmed new strength. They need to clear their recent highs to shift momentum.

— Bitcoin’s technical weakness needs confirmation —

Bitcoin hit $63K support and bounced slightly. However, the structure remains technically weak: series of lower highs and lower lows continues. It needs to make a higher high to signal reversal.

Many traders suspect Bitcoin might dive below $60K first before the bounce becomes real. Until Bitcoin shows genuine strength, it remains vulnerable. The bid is there at major support levels, but conviction hasn’t returned.

— Bottom line —

Nvidia is the fulcrum. If it clears $195, you’re looking at positive gamma acceleration and potential mechanical buying pressure. If it disappoints, software gets compressed further and the AI narrative gets challenged.

Flow is already showing late-cycle rotation. Energy crushing. Financials breaking down. Small caps outperforming. 

Commodities strong while financial assets weaken. The question is what comes next?

### Post #100: 2026-03-02 00:26

**Institutional Money Just Made One of the Biggest Energy Move in History And Most Traders Missed It**

While retail attention was scattered across earnings recaps and weekend headlines, the darkpool data quietly printed something that demands a closer look. One of the largest single institutional transactions ever recorded landed in the energy sector heading into Friday’s close. 

Oil surged now on open over the weekend, and energy names are set to open substantially higher Monday morning. 

— Follow the Flow, Not the Headlines —

Before diving into geopolitical narratives or news cycles, the actual order flow deserves attention first. 

The darkpool activity printed near Friday’s close, and oil has surged hard since.

This wasn’t coincidental. The second largest darkpool print in recorded history doesn’t materialize because algorithms misfired.

Every pullback has found footing at major demand zones, with technical setups consistently validating what the order flow was signaling.

The key observation is this that when institutional money deploys $5+ billion into energy at the close, concealment isn’t the objective. They’re establishing positioning ahead of something they believe carries real weight. Geopolitics provide the backdrop, but the flow is the actual mechanism.

— The S&P 500 Is Still Trapped Between Two Walls —

The S&P 500 remains confined within a critical range. 7,000 represents the major call wall on the upside. The put cluster sits between 6,800 and 6,770 on the downside. Neither level has cracked yet.

Until one of those walls gives way, no decisive directional claim can be made. The weekly 20-moving average continues to serve as the primary mean reversion reference. Sideways markets coil energy. When that compression breaks, the subsequent move tends to be explosive in whichever direction it chooses.

Options data reinforces this. Gamma walls at 7,000 and 6,800 are still containing the market. When these levels eventually give, dealer hedging mechanics can produce waterfall effects, particularly to the downside. Significant put hedges stacked up during Friday’s session, suggesting traders are bracing for a support failure scenario.

— Nvidia at 170: Watch This Level Closely —

Nvidia failed to push through a gamma wall and has been drifting lower under pressure. This development carries more weight than the earnings beat itself. The forward PE has compressed to some of the most attractive valuations seen in years, which historically represents a basing pattern rather than a breakdown.

The concern, however, is this: retail traders flooded into Nvidia following the earnings announcement. The highest-probability setups rarely cluster around the most crowded attention.

170 is the critical strike zone. Across every options expiration and every meaningful timeframe, 170 stands out as the major technical reference. A break below this level isn’t just a single-stock issue. 

Semiconductors underpin the entire AI narrative. A sustained move below 170 could indicate something more systemic is deteriorating, with downstream liquidity consequences across the broader tech complex.

For now, Nvidia is holding but it’s drifting. This level deserves close attention.

— Ghost Listings and the Bifurcated Labor Market —

Block announced meaningful workforce reductions, attributing them to AI-driven efficiency gains. Major tech firms are posting substantial job listings, but a significant portion appear to be ghost listings aka advertised roles with no active hiring behind them.

The real picture is more layered. Entry-level positions are disappearing at an accelerating pace, while skilled engineers continue commanding strong compensation and finding opportunities. This isn’t a uniform tech sector collapse it’s a bifurcated environment where the floor is being pulled from junior roles while experienced talent retains its premium.

This pattern doesn’t resemble what typically precedes a hard recessionary turn. It looks more like a productivity reallocation where capital and labor shifting toward higher-skilled output, compressing the entry tier in the process.
Bond flows and credit spreads deserve close monitoring from here. 

If real economic conditions deteriorate meaningfully, corporations can’t indefinitely sustain senior talent premiums. Debt markets would signal that inflection point well before equities do.

— Bitcoin: Demand Zone Holding, Structure Building —

Bitcoin tested support, dipped sharply, then recovered with conviction. Ethereum traced a nearly identical path, establishing meaningful structural levels in the process. The velocity of the recovery off those lows suggests genuine demand presence at lower prices.

IBIT, the Bitcoin ETF, is holding relatively well. The level to track going forward is 35. If Bitcoin continues pushing higher and clears the recent downtrend line, the setup favors another positive weekly close for crypto. This space warrants attention, particularly if broader risk-off sentiment begins to intensify.

— Key Levels Heading Into Next Week —

S&P 500: 7,000 is the upside call wall; 6,800 to 6,770 is the downside put cluster. A break of either level likely accelerates the move.

Nvidia: 170 is the pivotal technical and options reference. Hold or break here has broader sector implications.
Semiconductors — 376 is the sector-wide level to track.
Energy/Oil — 74 to 75 is the target zone. The move has already been significant — chasing at current levels isn’t the play.

Financials: JP Morgan and the broader financial sector deserve close attention. A breakdown in major financial support would signal real economic weakness, not just technical noise.

Treasury Yields: Dropped hard into Friday’s close. Continued weakness here would suggest the Fed may be forced to cut sooner than the market currently anticipates.

Bond Markets: This is the true signal. If corporate spreads widen materially, the economy is rolling over. Until the bond market shows genuine stress, assume the financial system’s plumbing remains functional we’re in a risk reallocation phase, not a crisis.

Next week’s critical tells: Does Nvidia hold 170? Do bonds stay stable? Does the S&P resolve its range? Those answers matter far more than any news cycle. War & News are all temporary. How many “war events” have we all been a part of now? I personally stopped counting.

### Post #102: 2026-03-08 13:18 — `$NVDA, $SMH, $IBIT, $SPY, $RSP, $QQQ`

**The Market Just Broke Something Important 🔻📉**

The S&P 500 closed beneath the weekly 20 moving average Friday. That does not happen often. And historically when it does, the next level that tends to get tested is the 50 moving average sitting around 6,500. That is about 200 points of downside from where the break occurred.

This was not just a bad jobs number. This was a confluence of multiple things cracking at once.

—The Jobs Picture—

Non-farm payrolls came in showing 92,000 jobs lost with unemployment sitting at 4.4%. But here is the part that does not get talked about enough: this is the 12th time since 1950 that we have seen this level of labor market weakness on a 6 month rolling basis. In the prior 11 instances, the US economy was already in recession. Revisions have consistently made these numbers worse after the fact. That is the pattern to watch, not the headline print.

—BlackRock Is Flashing Something—

BlackRock’s $26 billion private credit fund is now limiting redemptions to 5% of total assets against a 9.3% redemption request from investors. When a fund that size starts gating withdrawals, it is a signal that liquidity in private markets is tightening. This can feed into bonds and broader risk-on debt markets. It is a story worth watching closely through the rest of 2026.

—Options Structure Confirms the Breakdown—

Once $6,800 broke on the $SPY, the put wall exposure opened up. The majority of downside put positioning clusters around 6,500 and 6,600. Not a coincidence. That is where the structural demand likely lives. A 2050 cross also printed on the futures, which is rare and has historically preceded further downside on the few occasions it appeared.

On semis specifically, $SMH saw monstrous put flow come in near the end of last week. The options positioning suggests 360 to 370 is the key strike zone and that also happens to align with where the $SMH/SPY ratio hits 0.55, a level that would likely coincide with $SPY being around 6,600 if we get there.

$NVDA is holding 170 to 177 for now. That range remains the one to watch.

—Nasdaq and Software—

The $QQQ held $600 which is actually more constructive than the broader market. Tech and software have become the relative safe haven in this selloff, which is counterintuitive but makes sense when you consider funds that need to stay invested rotate toward beaten down names rather than adding new risk. $IGVB (software ETF) showed Wall Street quietly accumulating over the last few sessions even as the headline narrative turned negative on the sector.

Fund flows in $QQQ have been obliterated but that type of extreme outflow tends to show up near lows, not peaks. Worth keeping that in context.

—Oil Is a Real Problem Now—
Brent and WTI are spiking hard. Call volumes on oil were through the roof Friday. Kuwait crude production is down approximately 2.5% of global petroleum supply. Global jet fuel prices have tripled over a very short window.

Here is the issue: oil spiking creates an inflation problem at exactly the wrong time. The Fed is watching unemployment signal recession and watching energy signal renewed inflation simultaneously. The 2 year Treasury actually almost hit a new high after the jobs print, which is the opposite of what you would normally expect. 

That tension between weak growth and sticky inflation is what makes 2026 a genuinely difficult macro environment to navigate.

Oil hitting $105 and then potentially $110 a barrel is a realistic path if the current geopolitical situation does not de-escalate. But these spikes can reverse fast on any ceasefire news, so it is not a clean trade at these levels.

—Broader Market Structure—
Russell 2000 broke through key support at the 247 level and also lost its weekly 20. Equal weighted $RSP is near critical support with massive transaction clusters visible at the highs, suggesting significant distribution happened up there. High yield credit took out a support level as well. 

—Crypto—

Bitcoin on $IBIT is sitting just above the 38 zone and hovering. The most traded zone around 67 is where you want to see a bid show up if it pulls back further. No reaction on the lower timeframes yet.

—CPI Wednesday—

That is the next major data point. It will not capture the full inflationary impulse from the current oil spike but it will set the tone for how the Fed frames the next few weeks. Core PCE after that is what the Fed actually uses as its primary gauge.

The structure of this market now requires patience. The flows are negative, the technical damage is real, and the macro backdrop is genuinely complicated. That does not mean every long is broken but it does mean being selective and sizing accordingly until the key levels either hold or give way completely.

### Post #106: 2026-03-15 23:31 — `$NVDA, $IGV, $XLF, $IBIT, $SPY, $IAU, $QQQ, $TSLA`

**The market is getting more and more hectic….**

The 50 weekly moving average on $SPY is sitting right around 6500.

That is the next line in the sand.

When markets lose the 20 weekly, the historical pattern is clear. You drop, you rally back to test the 20 as resistance, you fail, and then you move toward the 50. That sequence is playing out right now in real time. We are in the middle of it.

–The Debt Problem Nobody Wants To Talk About–

$300 billion in private debt is starting to show cracks.
JP Morgan’s Dimon has a saying: where there’s one cockroach, there’s usually more. Wells Fargo is reportedly the most exposed of the major banks to this private debt stress and it was also the worst performing bank this week. That is not a coincidence.

Financials have now broken below the 50 area on $XLF and are trending toward the 46 zone with no obvious technical buy support visible right now. Corporate bonds dropped this week. High yield junk dropped even harder. This is the first meaningful flinch from the bond market in almost a year.

–The Worst March Since 1950–

Through the first 10 trading days, this is the ninth worst start to March since 1950. Currently sitting around 3.59% down.
Historical data on this setup shows that when losses in the first 10 days stay under 5%, markets tend to stabilize by month end. Consolidation is the base case. A straight recovery is not.

The $QQQ fell below the 200 day moving average for the first time in 8 months. That is a different market than what most people were positioned for. Importantly though, the NASDAQ held 24K and has not yet made a lower low. That matters.

Weekend futures currently show  less panic than the headlines suggested.

–Where The Options Flow Points–

The put walls are stacked. We are in negative gamma, which means dealer hedging amplifies moves lower rather than dampening them.

Key levels to watch on $SPY: 6500 is the most heavily struck put zone across multiple expiration windows and lines up almost exactly with the 50 weekly. 6700 and 6600 are the intermediate zones before we get there.

On $QQQ the major put concentration that was sitting at 600 and 595 has already been breached. Next downside zone the flow is pointing toward is 585.

$NVDA holding 180 keeps the broader index picture from getting worse. Semis are the backbone. $TSLA sitting between 400 call wall and 380 put wall. The daily 20 has been acting as resistance on every bounce on Tesla, not a clean chart right now.

Dark pool cluster prints appeared near the close on a major $SPY fund at the end of the week, which could be early signs of institutional dip buying. Not confirmation. Just something to watch.

–Oil, Gold, And The Inflation Picture–

The oil to gold ratio just broke its long term downward trend line for the first time in years. Oil is still historically cheap relative to gold and that gap is starting to close.

Crude futures are holding elevated with a higher high forming. The December crude contract is holding near 73. If oil works back toward 100 (which it already is doing in night hours) a barrel and sustains there, inflation expectations reprice significantly. A break above 120 would be a different conversation entirely.

Gold had a poor weekly close and rallied back to where you would typically expect resistance. $IAU just saw record market outflows per flow data, and separately saw what appears to be the largest ever single transaction on record coming through. Generally when you see outflows this large it either means you are near a low or approaching base support. Not there yet technically. Expect a period of sideways action on gold before the next directional move.

The US dollar has been strengthening for two weeks and is now pushing toward the 125 level. A break above that would set a higher high and further support the case for yields running higher. Rate hike expectations are climbing globally. 

Australia is pricing in two to three hikes on a hot economy combined with rising energy costs. The 2 year yield spiked on the latest inflation read and the 10 year is forming what looks like a base potentially setting up for a sustained move higher.

A new Fed chair is coming soon. The question the market is quietly starting to price is whether they cut into a weakening economy anyway and risk runaway inflation, or hold and let the economy absorb the pressure. Neither answer is clean.

–Safe Havens And What Is Actually Holding–

The US stock market is now only the fifth best performing market globally. Capital is rotating toward perceived safe havens, which is historically common when uncertainty spikes. 

South Korea is a market worth watching closely this year after running approximately 160 to 180% in around 12 months. That kind of move followed by volatility entering the room is usually a signal the easy part of that trade is over.
The Kospi and Nikkei are both at potential technical tops. Worth monitoring closely over the next few weeks.

$IBIT and the broader crypto complex are holding better than almost any other risk asset right now. Assets that already absorbed heavy drawdowns earlier are acting as relative safe havens when volatility picks up. 

Same dynamic is showing up in $IGV software, which is holding relatively well considering the broader market weakness, precisely because it was already beaten down.
Bitcoin held above the 72K breakout level. Chart looks constructive relative to everything else.

Chinese markets are holding the 25,000 level and basing better than you would expect given what is happening in US markets.

–The Week Ahead–

The Fed decision Wednesday at 2 PM ET is the centerpiece of the week. Not just the rate decision but the statement and any commentary around inflation tolerance and forward guidance. PPI data also hits this week and will set the tone going into the Fed. Canadian CPI and Australian cash rate decision also on the calendar.

Watch 6500 on $SPY. Watch $XLF 46. Watch crude for any acceleration toward 100. Watch the dollar at 125. And watch what the Fed signals about their tolerance for inflation versus growth.

The data will keep telling us what to do. Stay patient and stay sized appropriately.

On a side note I will be doing more play posts soon using Trader Daddy Pro our new options flow platform. Stay tuned…

### Post #107: 2026-03-19 01:26 — `$NVDA, $IVV, $SPY, $QQQ, $TSLA`

**Is This a Race for Cash? Breaking Down the Worst Market Day of 2026**

Every sector closed red today. Not most sectors. All of them.

That is a sell everything session, and they are rare. When the market stops being selective and just dumps everything indiscriminately, it is telling you something important. The question is what to do with that information.

Here is everything that matters right now.

—THE FED PULLED THE TRIGGER—

The catalyst was Powell. As usual, the real volatility did not arrive at the rate decision itself but roughly 30 minutes into the press conference. That is almost a pattern at this point.

The headline everyone needed to hear: rate cut expectations for 2026 have been gutted. We went from pricing in multiple cuts this year to essentially zero, maybe one in the best case scenario. That repricing is enormous and it is now flowing through every asset class globally.

Powell pushed back on stagflation concerns during the presser, but the debate is far from settled. Some are making the case for deflation instead. Either way, the combination of stubborn oil prices and a Fed that has no room to cut is the setup that tends to create late cycle stress. Australia has already absorbed two rate hikes this year. Global yields are climbing in lockstep. This is not a US story in isolation.

Worth noting: midterm election years historically see up to two significant dips and carry elevated volatility heading into the election itself. That seasonal context matters when sizing risk right now.

—BONDS ARE THE STORY—

Both the 2 year and 10 year yields broke out today. The 10 year is pushing back toward its recent highs. BofA high yield spreads just printed a new high, meaning credit markets are actively repricing risk right now, not lagging it.

When you look at IEF as a way to strip out the yield component and just observe price structure, the level it is approaching is critical. A break there signals genuine bond market stress, not just a yield move.

Here is the historical context that matters: when bond yields are in a rising trend, the average annual return on the S&P 500 drops to roughly 7%. That is not a crash call. But it is a meaningful headwind that changes how you should be thinking about risk/reward on long equity exposure.

The bond market flinching while the equity market is already under pressure from a rate repricing is a different environment than anything we have seen in the last couple of years.

—THE DOLLAR IS WAKING UP—

Remember when cash was trash? When dollar death was the consensus? When demonetization was the theme everyone was pushing?

The US dollar is forming a clean W style base right now. The critical level to watch is the 103 to 104 zone. If that breaks to the upside, it aligns almost precisely with the 6,500 to 6,600 range on the S&P where all the major technical and options structure is sitting.

That convergence is not coincidence. A dollar breakout while equities are testing major support creates what tends to be a cascade effect. Risk gets repriced across equities, commodities, and crypto simultaneously. Cash literally becomes the trade.

This is the chart that ties everything else together right now.

—ENERGY, COMMODITIES, AND WHAT IT MEANS FOR EVERYONE—

Oil is still hovering around 100 a barrel. Not blowing out dramatically but not backing off either.

The historical relationship between surging energy prices and recession probability is well documented. 2022 was the exception where that signal did not fully trigger a bear market, though markets did sell off hard and we came close to a technical trend change. The important detail from that episode: 60 to 100 days following a major geopolitical or oil shock is typically when you find out whether markets have bottomed or whether the selling accelerates.

We are early in that window right now.

The secondary effect that does not get enough attention: when gas prices run, food and agricultural prices follow. That relationship is consistent across cycles. If this oil level holds or extends, the consumer is going to feel it at the grocery store. That creates a feedback loop into economic data that the Fed will have to navigate.

The commodity flow through generally runs gold and silver first, then copper, then energy stocks, then agriculture. Copper has struggled over the last 24 hours. Agricultural names have actually been quietly moving for a few weeks now. Worth tracking that rotation.

—KEY TECHNICAL LEVELS—

This is where it gets specific.

$SPY / S&P 500: The 6,500 level is the line in the sand. That is where the weekly 50 moving average sits. It is where major put walls are stacked. And a clean weekly close below that level would technically constitute a change of trend on the weekly chart, which is meaningful. The 6,500 to 6,600 zone is where the market decides whether this is a correction or something more serious. Below 6,500 and things can move fast.

The 20/50 moving average sequence played out largely as expected: broke the 20, bounced back to retest it, now approaching the 50. That is textbook structure. The question is whether the 50 holds.

$QQQ: Key support at 580 and 575. Less clean than the S&P setup but the same logic applies. 24,000 on the NASDAQ is the level where you start to see broader structural support converge.

$NVDA and semiconductors: Still holding above 170 on Nvidia. The semiconductor index is still above 370 to 377. This matters because semis are the backbone of the index right now.

$TSLA: Key zone at 380. Not the primary focus in this environment. S&P, dollar, and bonds are the three things to watch right now.

Gold: No clean entry thesis until structure develops on the smaller timeframes. Patience required.

$RUT / Russell 2000: Has not found technical support yet. That is a yellow flag for broader market health since small caps tend to lead risk off moves.

—CRYPTO—

Bitcoin pulled back to its anchored VWAP from the recent low. That is typically where you want to see base structure begin forming on the smaller timeframes.

The data point worth paying attention to: the 30 day Bitcoin ETF flow has actually rolled from negative to positive. Cumulative flows have improved even as price has pulled back over the last 24 hours. That divergence between price and flow is something to monitor. It suggests real demand starting to build at this demand zone rather than pure capitulation selling.

Ethereum followed a similar path, broke above a minor resistance level, ran into supply, and pulled back to the daily 20 and anchored VWAP. That is where structure should start to form if there is any near term recovery setup.

—QUADRUPLE WITCHING THIS FRIDAY—

This cannot be understated as a risk factor for the rest of the week.

Quadruple witching means index futures, index options, single stock futures, and single stock options all expire simultaneously. The amount of capital at stake makes the options market structure extremely relevant to price action.

The 6,500 and 6,600 strikes on the S&P are showing up with massive activity. That concentration of puts in this zone means two things: there is structural support there as market makers defend those levels, but if 6,500 gives way, the options flow flips and can amplify the downside quickly.

Large institutional transactions came through $IVV, one of the biggest S&P ETFs, in clusters right before the market dropped. That timing is worth noting when thinking about where smart money was positioning.

Thursday and Friday this week carry outsized volatility risk. Manage size accordingly.

—MARKET BREADTH—

The market has been running with deteriorating breadth for months but today was the first session in a while where it was genuinely a sell everything environment rather than a rotation.

It is not a crash indicator on its own but combined with the bond stress, the dollar setup, the options structure, and the quadruple witching context, it adds to a picture that says this week is not a normal week.

Financials are the worst performing sector of 2026. That is rare. It almost never happens near the top of a market without carrying some significance. The fact that the trend has not technically broken yet makes the setup even more interesting to watch.

—THE FRAMEWORK GOING FORWARD—

When VIX pushes above 20 to 25, the playbook is to simplify. Stop watching 40 tickers and start watching four things: S&P 500, US dollar, bond yields, and credit spreads. Everything else is noise until those four resolve.

Right now all four are at inflection points simultaneously. That does not happen often.

React to what the levels tell you. The 6,500 to 6,600 zone on the S&P is the first real test. A weekly 50 hold would be constructive. A break below 6,500 into quadruple witching changes the calculus significantly.

The edge right now is not predicting what happens next. It is having the levels defined in advance and being ready to react when the market tells you which way it is going.

### Post #109: 2026-03-26 04:08

**The Market Has No Leader Right Now and That’s the Real Problem 👇👇👇**

When every stock that carried this bull run is down double digits from its peak simultaneously, something structural is shifting. Not a rotation. Not a sector dip. A leadership vacuum.

Apple down 11%. Nvidia down 13%. Google and Amazon both 16%. Meta 24%. Microsoft 31.6%. Tesla in a full technical bear market from its highs. Over $3 trillion in market cap gone across those names alone.
Yet the S&P is only down around 5%. That gap tells you everything.

— Why the S&P Is Masking the Damage —

Mag 7 and tech represent over 35% of the index. When that much concentration starts breaking down, the headline number lies to you. What’s kept it propped is late cycle rotation, money moving out of growth and into other areas as a survival mechanism. 

Rotation is not a bullish signal. It’s a defensive one.
The other thing masking the damage is that the Federal Reserve cut rates last year, which bought time. That time may be running out.

— Bonds Are the Tell —

High yield spreads just made a new high. That’s the bond market pricing in elevated risk on debt and it doesn’t do that casually. The MOVE Index, which tracks bond market volatility, remains at extreme levels with no sign of compression.

Here’s the framework: when you want to know if a market is genuinely stabilizing, you need VIX crushing back down AND the MOVE Index crushing back down simultaneously, with price breaking higher highs. 

That’s cross-correlation confirming a real bottom. Right now neither is happening.

The VIX has been above 20 for 18 consecutive days. Once that stretches past 50 days historically, the associated drawdowns get significantly larger. We’re not there. But 18 days is already crossing thresholds that matter.

When bonds and equities are both expressing uncertainty at the same time, follow the bond market. Bond traders understand systemic debt risk better than equity markets do.

— The US Dollar and the Cash Rotation Setup —

Six months ago the consensus was dollar dead, cash trash, dollar demonetization. The chart doesn’t agree with that right now. The dollar is building what looks like a base at a critical level. If it breaks to the upside, that’s a signal that real money is rotating into cash as a hedge, not into risk assets.

A rising dollar in this environment combined with elevated credit spreads and a high MOVE Index is the early anatomy of something more serious than a garden variety pullback.

— What the S&P Chart Is Actually Saying —

Three long-legged doji candles in a row around the key support zone. That’s a market with zero directional conviction, waiting for a news catalyst to decide. Higher time frames remain a series of lower lows and lower highs. 

The downtrend is intact.

6,500 is the level that keeps appearing across every options dataset. It’s the dominant put wall, clustered with activity across multiple expirations, and it sits near the weekly 50 moving average, one of the most technically significant lines on the board right now.

The NASDAQ is at 587 on QQQ with put concentration sitting at 580. The entire market is hovering just above its own put walls.

If 6,500 breaks on the S&P, the structure for a cascade effect is in place.

— Intraday Reversals Tell You the Market Is Lost —

Intraday reversals have been spiking. Markets gapping down and closing up. Opening strong and closing weak. Session after session. A market printing that pattern has no conviction. It’s purely news driven, reacting to headlines in real time with no underlying directional thesis.

In prior supply disruption environments, the first 60 to 100 days has historically been where markets either find a genuine floor or deteriorate significantly further. We are early in that window. The longer the disruption extends, the worse the historical outcomes have been.

— Commodities & Food are looking bad  —

Energy leads, food follows. Fertilizer stocks have moved hard and when they sustain elevated levels, food staples feel it with a lag. Staples as a sector is now threatening its monthly 50 EMA, a level that has held every major pullback since the global financial crisis. It’s down 10% from peak and approaching a zone that, if broken, would suggest the market is pricing in something beyond a standard correction.

That is a chart worth watching very closely. It’s a read on how the street views the health of the American consumer.

On gold: nine consecutive down days and sitting at the 200 day moving average alongside prior demand zones. 

Historically that setup has produced supportive action. The selloff itself is mechanical. Gold is a currency and when rate cut expectations reprice lower, gold becomes less attractive on a relative basis. The market went from pricing multiple cuts in 2026 to basically none. That repricing hit gold hard.

— Where the Market Is Actually Holding —

Semiconductors are the line. Nvidia above 170. 
SOX holding 370 to 377. If semis stay intact into the next earnings cycle, the market is still giving the AI capex story the benefit of the doubt even while questioning near-term profitability.

Software hit a major demand zone last session with heavy volume. Bitcoin is holding and printing a potential inverse head and shoulders on the smaller time frames. Those two have tracked each other closely and both sitting at structure is worth noting.

— Where We Are —

This is not 2023. It is not 2024. Tech is not riding to the rescue. 

Financials are the worst performing sector of the year, which is historically rare and tends to carry a specific warning when it occurs near cycle peaks. 

Bond risk is real. Concentration is unwinding. And a news-driven market with no directional conviction is one of the hardest environments to navigate.
